# Project Progress & Handoff

Full state of the CropX website rebuild as of **August 2, 2026**. Use this as a context primer for any new Claude session so we never lose progress.

## September 16, 2026 — New block: Content Card Carousel (cropx/cards-carousel)

Lauren shared a screenshot of Ambrook's customer-story carousel (wide card, photo left, name/quote/stat right, arrow nav) and asked how easy it'd be to build something similar that pulls the same data as the existing 3-Column Content Cards block. Clarified scope with her via three quick questions before building: (1) new standalone block vs. a layout toggle on the existing Cards block → **new block**, keeping the heavily-used Cards block untouched; (2) manual arrows/dots only vs. an auto-advance option → **auto-advance option** (off by default, editor toggle); (3) identical fields to Cards vs. an added stat/number overlay → **add a stat overlay** field per card.

**What it is:** `cropx/cards-carousel` ("Content Card Carousel") — a horizontal scroll-snap carousel of wide cards (photo left ~42%, title/tag/date/excerpt/CTA right), one card in focus at a time with a deliberate peek of the neighboring card at each edge. Same three content-source modes as Cards (Manual / Choose Posts / Auto — Auto has Single-query and Multiple-sources sub-modes), same reorder-by-drag sidebar UI, same taxonomy/category filters. This is a genuine sibling of Cards, not a fork with drifted behavior — the query engine (manual/posts/auto-single/auto-mixed resolution logic) is a straight port of Cards' render.php logic, just under a new `cropx_cards_carousel_card_from_post()` helper (deliberately NOT reusing Cards' `cropx_card_from_post()` name, so the two blocks never collide via PHP's `function_exists()` guard if both appear on one page).

**New vs. Cards:**
- **Layout**: one wide `.cc-card` per carousel position (flex row: `.cc-card-photo` ~42% width / `.cc-card-body` remainder) instead of a 3-column grid. Carousel mechanics (scroll-snap track, arrow buttons, dot indicators, resize-aware `visibleCount()`/`maxIndex()` maths) are a straight copy of Testimonials Carousel's `tcarousel` controller, renamed `cccarousel` and it just naturally resolves to ~1 visible card instead of ~3 given each card's width.
- **Stat badge** (e.g. "$12M in credit secured", inspired directly by the Ambrook reference): optional `statValue`/`statLabel` pair per card, rendered as white text over a bottom-of-photo dark gradient scrim (`.cc-stat-badge`) — no boxed badge, matches the reference's "text directly on photo" look. Only available in Manual and Choose Posts modes (added to `cards[]` and `manualPosts[]` slot shapes) — Auto mode never shows one, since there's no sensible automatic source for a curated stat number on a generic latest-posts feed. Documented this limitation directly in render.php's doc comment so it isn't mistaken for a bug later.
- **Auto-advance**: `autoAdvance` boolean attribute + sidebar toggle, wired through `data-cc-auto-advance` on the section and the existing shared `initSnapAutoAdvance()` helper (`src/shared/autoAdvance.js` — already used by Photo Carousel and Testimonials Carousel, no changes needed there).

**Editor canvas difference worth knowing:** the editor renders cards stacked full-width (`.cc-editor-stack`) rather than the real scroll-snap track — RichText fields inside a horizontally-scrolling flex track are awkward to reach in a narrow editor iframe, and there's no real carousel behavior an editor needs to preview by scrolling sideways. The front end always uses the real `cccarousel` track regardless of what the editor shows.

**Files created** (`src/blocks/cards-carousel/`): `block.json`, `index.js`, `edit.js` (deliberate close port of `cards/edit.js` — see its own doc comment for the exact list of differences), `render.php`, `style.css`, `editor.css`, `view.js`. All verified for syntax: `block.json` JSON-parses; `index.js`/`view.js` pass `node --check`; `edit.js` (JSX, can't `node --check` directly) and `render.php` verified via brace/paren/bracket balance; `style.css`/`editor.css` verified via brace balance. Auto-registered via `inc/blocks.php`'s `glob()`-based block discovery — no `functions.php` changes needed. No `"version"` key in `block.json` needed either, matching every other block in this project — `inc/enqueue.php`'s `block_type_metadata` filter injects `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` automatically (CLAUDE.md gotcha #10).

**Local build environment was broken sitewide — root cause found and fixed.** Lauren's first `npm run build` attempt this session appeared to hang for 15+ minutes, then silently exited with code 0 without touching `build/` at all (confirmed via stale `build/` mtime and a missing `build/blocks/cards-carousel/`). Ruled out, in order: watch-mode confusion, no live node process actually running, wrong working directory, Node v24 vs. old `@wordpress/scripts` v27.9.0 mismatch (installed `nvm`, tried Node 20.20.2 — build still silently no-opped), and "Local" (Local by Flywheel) not running (irrelevant — `npm run build` is pure filesystem/webpack, no WP/PHP/MySQL dependency).

**Actual root cause:** iCloud Drive's "Desktop & Documents Folders" sync, which covers `~/Documents` (where this repo lives), was stuck in a broken state — `brctl status` showed a live CloudKit error (`"Error uploading asset: Quota exceeded"`, `CKRetryAfter = 324`): Lauren's iCloud storage is full and sync was endlessly retrying, intercepting filesystem operations on the repo's large `node_modules`/`.git` tree. This is the same underlying failure mode as this Cowork sandbox's own long-standing "Resource deadlock avoided" errors on this repo (the sandbox's workspace mount is the same iCloud-synced folder).

**Fix:** relocated the whole project out of iCloud's reach — `rsync -av --exclude='node_modules' --exclude='node_modules 2' ~/Documents/github/cropx-website-2026/ ~/Sites/cropx-website-2026/` (a plain `mv` was tried first and itself hung, consistent with iCloud's file-provider layer intercepting the syscall; rsync excluding the disposable `node_modules` completed cleanly). Then `cd ~/Sites/cropx-website-2026/wp-theme/cropx && npm install && npm run build` — succeeded in 2071ms (webpack 5.106.2), correctly compiling `cards-carousel/view.js + 2 modules`.

**⚠️ Going forward: `~/Sites/cropx-website-2026` is now Lauren's real working copy.** The old `~/Documents/github/cropx-website-2026` copy (which this Cowork session is connected to) is stale and no longer being built into — Lauren needs to either point future Cowork sessions at the new `~/Sites/` path, or keep rsync'ing fresh `build/` output back to the old path when a Cowork session needs to read it (done manually for this deploy — see below). This should be resolved properly before the next block build.

**Deployed to staging:** with the fresh build copied back into the old path, packaged and delivered:
- `cards-carousel-deploy-v0.4.76.zip` — `build/blocks/cards-carousel/` (11 files) + `functions.php` (version bumped `0.4.75` → `0.4.76`). Upload via WP File Manager and extract over `wp-content/themes/cropx/` (small targeted zip, not a full-theme re-upload, per CLAUDE.md's staging-deploy convention).
- `cards-carousel-revert-v0.4.76.zip` — since this is a brand-new block with nothing on staging to restore, "revert" here just means: (1) extract this zip's `functions.php` to restore `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.75`, and (2) manually delete the `wp-content/themes/cropx/build/blocks/cards-carousel/` folder via File Manager. There's no prior version of this block's files to roll back to.

## September 16, 2026 (later) — Cards Carousel: full-bleed viewport-centered layout

After seeing the deployed block, Lauren asked for two layout changes to match Ambrook's actual reference more closely: (1) the carousel should break out of the section's normal content width so peeking neighbor cards get cut off by the actual browser edge, not by a content-container edge, and (2) the focused card should be VISUALLY centered on the page (not just "in focus" within a boxed track), with circular prev/next arrows floating directly on that centered card's left/right edge rather than sitting in a control bar underneath.

**Full-bleed breakout:** `.cccarousel` now uses the standard `width: 100vw; margin-left/right: calc(50% - 50vw);` technique to escape `.cc-inner`'s max-width/padding — this cancels out algebraically regardless of the parent's width, so it always spans the true viewport edge to edge. `.cc-section` already had `overflow-x: clip`, so the usual ~15px scrollbar-width rounding never produces a page-level horizontal scrollbar.

**True viewport-centered focus + edge-anchored arrows:** introduced a single `--cc-card-w` custom property on `.cccarousel` that everything else derives from — the track's `padding-inline`/`scroll-padding-inline` (`calc(50% - var(--cc-card-w)/2)`, letting even the first/last cards scroll to true center), each card's `flex-basis`, and the arrow buttons' `left` offset (`calc(50% ∓ var(--cc-card-w)/2)` + `translate(-50%,-50%)` to center the button itself on that edge point). Since all four things read from the same variable, they can never drift out of sync when the responsive breakpoints override it (`clamp(320px, 64vw, 1040px)` desktop → `min(560px, 84vw)` at ≤900px → `min(380px, 84vw)` at ≤600px, the last with a smaller 36px arrow so it can't poke past the edge on narrow phones).

**Arrow buttons moved into `.cccarousel-viewport`** (not `.cccarousel` directly) so their `top:50%` centers on the card row's own height specifically — the dots row (`.cccarousel-controls`) still sits below, back inside the normal `.cc-inner` max-width, without affecting that math. view.js needed zero changes: it does `root.querySelector('.cccarousel-prev'/'.cccarousel-dots')` where `root` is `.cccarousel`, and both elements are still descendants of it regardless of which level they're nested at.

**Files changed (src only — editor canvas uses the separate `.cc-editor-stack` stacked layout per edit.js and never renders the real `.cccarousel` track, so no edit.js/editor.css changes needed):**
- `src/blocks/cards-carousel/style.css` — rewrote the "Carousel shell" section (`.cccarousel`/`.cccarousel-viewport`/`.cccarousel-track`/arrow positioning), simplified `.cccarousel-controls` to dots-only, updated the responsive breakpoints to redeclare `--cc-card-w` instead of separate flex-basis rules. Verified via brace/paren balance (98/98, 130/130).
- `src/blocks/cards-carousel/render.php` — moved the prev/next `<button>`s to be children of `.cccarousel-viewport` (siblings of `.cccarousel-track`), leaving `.cccarousel-controls` holding only `.cccarousel-dots`. Verified via brace/paren/div-tag balance (33/33, 198/198, 11 open/11 close).

**Not yet built/deployed.** Lauren's local build environment is fixed as of today's earlier iCloud-relocation work, but her real working copy is now `~/Sites/cropx-website-2026` while these src edits landed in the old `~/Documents/github/cropx-website-2026` (the path this Cowork session is connected to) — she needs to sync just this block's two changed files over before rebuilding:

```bash
rsync -av \
  ~/Documents/github/cropx-website-2026/wp-theme/cropx/src/blocks/cards-carousel/ \
  ~/Sites/cropx-website-2026/wp-theme/cropx/src/blocks/cards-carousel/

cd ~/Sites/cropx-website-2026/wp-theme/cropx
npm run build
```

Then hard-refresh the editor and check the front end (published page, not the editor canvas, since the editor intentionally shows the stacked preview) for the full-bleed centered layout. Once confirmed, the usual small-targeted-zip staging deploy (functions.php version bump + build/blocks/cards-carousel/) can follow.

## September 15, 2026 (even later) — Wire scroll-reveal into Dealer Finder block

Lauren asked which blocks still lack scroll-reveal ("What other blocks do we have left to animate? Any?"). Dealer Finder was the only genuine gap — everything else without it (hero family, nav/footer/menu-links/footer-brand) is deliberately excluded. She said: "Okay, let's try it but make it easy to revert."

**Approach, same pattern as every other block in the scroll-reveal rollout:**
- `.df-block` (the block wrapper) is both the observed root passed to `initScrollReveal('.df-block')` in `view.js`, and carries `reveal-group` directly (not just a descendant), so the wrapper's own reveal happens via the `.reveal-group.is-revealed .reveal-item` "self" case.
- `reveal-up` + staggered `--reveal-delay` on `.df-sidebar-header` (0.05s), `.df-search-wrap` (0.15s), and `.df-map-wrap` (0.1s).
- `reveal-item` on the server-rendered loading `<li>` (0.2s) so it doesn't sit stark before the JS list arrives.
- Standard reveal CSS block copied verbatim into `style.css` (same bytes used sitewide — copy/paste is required since `@import` of the shared CSS file isn't webpack-compatible in this project's build).

**The interesting bit — dynamically-populated list:** Dealer Finder's `<ul class="df-list">` is populated by `view.js`'s `fetch()` call to the dealer REST API, not server-rendered in a PHP loop like every other block using this system. Worked through this from CSS first principles rather than copying a pattern from elsewhere: once `.df-block` already has `is-revealed` (which happens almost immediately, since the block usually starts in/near the viewport), any `<li class="df-item reveal-item">` that `renderList()` later inserts into the DOM automatically starts matching `.reveal-group.is-revealed .reveal-item` the instant it's added, and CSS begins its animation right then — no extra JS re-trigger call needed. This means every dealer-list item fades in on initial load, AND every time the list is repopulated by a zip search or "use my location" click, which reads as a pleasant "results refreshed" cue for free. Gave each `<li>` a staggered delay via `--reveal-delay: (min(index,8) * 0.05)s` so the list cascades in rather than popping all at once, capped at index 8 so a long results list doesn't drag the animation out.

**Files changed (src, all verified — JS `node --check`, PHP brace/paren balance, CSS brace balance):**
- `src/blocks/dealer-finder/render.php` — doc comment explaining the approach; `reveal-group` added to wrapper class; `reveal-up`+delay on sidebar-header/search-wrap/map-wrap; `reveal-item`+delay on the loading `<li>`.
- `src/blocks/dealer-finder/view.js` — `import { initScrollReveal }` + `initScrollReveal('.df-block')` call; in `renderList()`'s forEach, added `reveal-item` class and `--reveal-delay` inline style per dealer `<li>`.
- `src/blocks/dealer-finder/style.css` — standard reveal CSS block inserted after the doc comment.

**Build propagation (hand-patched, `wp-scripts build` still broken in this sandbox — "Resource deadlock avoided"):**
- `build/blocks/dealer-finder/render.php` — copied verbatim from the updated src (this file isn't webpack-compiled, so it's a straight copy, unlike view.js/style.css).
- `build/blocks/dealer-finder/view.js` — two surgical string edits to the existing minified blob rather than a full rewrite (lower risk of a transcription error across ~400 lines of dense minified logic): (1) prepended a small standalone IIFE implementing the IntersectionObserver reveal-on-`.df-block` logic, matching the pattern already used elsewhere; (2) changed the `renderList` forEach signature to take an index (named `_i` deliberately — avoids colliding with the single-letter `esc()` helper `t` already in scope) and added the `reveal-item` class + `--reveal-delay` style.
- `build/blocks/dealer-finder/style-index.css` — prepended the same minified reveal CSS block used on every other block's compiled CSS (byte-identical to e.g. zoho-contact-form's).
- All three build files verified: `node --check` on view.js, brace/paren balance on render.php, brace balance on style-index.css.

**Version + delivery:**
- `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.74` → `0.4.75` in `functions.php`.
- Packaged `dealer-finder-reveal-deploy.zip` (functions.php + build/blocks/dealer-finder/{render.php,view.js,style-index.css}) and `dealer-finder-reveal-revert.zip` (same 4 files at their pre-change state — the pre-change build files were reconstructed programmatically by reversing the exact string edits above, then verified clean of any "reveal" references and passing `node --check`).
- Delivered both zips to Lauren via WP File Manager instructions (extract into `wp-content/themes/cropx/`, same small-targeted-zip convention as every prior patch — see CLAUDE.md's "Deploying to live staging" section).
- **Not yet confirmed live** — next session should verify the reveal animation fires on staging's Dealer Finder page (both the static sidebar/map panels and the dynamically-loaded dealer list).

## September 15, 2026 (later) — Contact form "isn't displaying": sitewide scroll-reveal safety net

**Report.** Lauren: the homepage contact form (zoho-contact-form block, `.zcf-section`) wasn't displaying. Confirmed it wasn't a caching issue — she'd already hard-refreshed and still saw the problem.

**Diagnosis.** The block itself and its content were never missing — inspected the live DOM directly and `.zcf-section` was present with full, correct content (~32KB of real HTML: heading, intro body, the whole form). The block's own scroll-reveal wiring (task #701, round 4) was also verified correct on both ends: pulled the actual deployed `build/blocks/zoho-contact-form/view.js` and `style-index.css` straight from new.cropx.com and diffed them against source — byte-for-byte correct, observer watches `.zcf-section`, CSS correctly cascades `is-revealed` to the heading/body/card, script is `defer`red so it always runs after the DOM exists.

The actual bug is a gap in `src/shared/scrollReveal.js` itself, not anything block-specific: every `reveal-up`/`reveal-item` element across the site starts at `opacity:0` (via `scroll-reveal.css`) and stays that way forever unless its block's own `IntersectionObserver` fires and adds `is-revealed`. There is no fallback. Browsers are allowed to delay or pause `IntersectionObserver` callbacks for a tab that isn't actually visible — backgrounded, minimized, or (on some platforms) fully covered by another window, even while it technically has focus. Reproduced this directly: in an automated test tab whose `document.visibilityState` was `"hidden"`, a brand-new `IntersectionObserver` watching an element already sitting in the viewport never fired at all — not even its normal first callback. A live screenshot of the page in that state showed exactly Lauren's symptom: a blank taupe gap where the heading/body/form card should be, all three stuck at `opacity:0`. Since nothing else ever adds `is-revealed`, a visitor unlucky enough to hit this (tab was backgrounded on load, window got covered while scrolling, slow/throttled device, etc.) is left with a permanently invisible section — not a transient glitch, a dead end.

**Fix chosen: one sitewide fallback script, not a patch to all 38 blocks.** `src/shared/scrollReveal.js` is imported into 38 blocks' `view.js` files and — because this theme's build pipeline only hand-propagates changes to `build/` (npm run build is broken in this sandbox; see CLAUDE.md's "Deploying to live staging" section) — webpack bundles a separate inlined copy of the reveal logic into each of those 38 compiled files. Patching the actual root cause into all 38 would mean manually hand-editing 38+ already-minified files (69 total `initScrollReveal()` call sites across them, several blocks calling it twice for independent subsections) — a huge, error-prone blast radius for one shared behavior.

Instead: a new independent file, `assets/js/scroll-reveal-safety-net.js`, enqueued sitewide and unconditionally (same pattern as `cropx-image-caption-width`, right next to it in `inc/enqueue.php`). It doesn't know or care which selector any given block originally observed — it only needs the shared CSS contract every block already uses (`reveal-up` / `reveal-group` / `reveal-item` / `is-revealed`), so it covers all 38 blocks at once with zero per-block changes. It periodically/eventfully sweeps for anything still stuck hidden that's actually sitting in the viewport and reveals it directly:
- `visibilitychange` (tab becomes visible again — the exact moment a throttled observer would've been starved), `pageshow`, `focus`, and a throttled `scroll` listener as event-driven triggers.
- A capped poll (every 500ms, up to 12 checks / 6 seconds) as a last-resort catch-all in case none of those fire.
- Self-cancels (removes the scroll listener, stops the poll) once nothing is left un-revealed anywhere on the page, so it costs nothing once the normal fast path has done its job — which is still the overwhelming majority of the time.

**Not a block change** — `assets/js/` isn't part of the webpack build (confirmed: `wp-scripts build` still fails in this sandbox with "Resource deadlock avoided", same as before; `assets/js/*.js` files are served directly, not compiled), so no `build/` propagation step was needed, unlike a block CSS/JS change.

**Shipped:** bumped `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.74` (was `0.4.73` locally / `0.4.72` live — the 0.4.73 bump was already sitting in the repo from some other not-yet-deployed local change; skipped past it rather than reuse it). Two files touched (`functions.php` for the version bump, `inc/enqueue.php` to enqueue the new script) plus the one new file. Verified: `node --check` on the new JS, brace/paren balance on both PHP files, and a diff of deploy vs. revert confirming only the intended lines changed.

**Delivered to Lauren:** `scroll-reveal-safety-net-deploy.zip` (functions.php + inc/enqueue.php + assets/js/scroll-reveal-safety-net.js — extract over the theme root in WP File Manager) and `scroll-reveal-safety-net-revert.zip` (original functions.php + inc/enqueue.php, no safety-net file to remove since it's additive) as the rollback pair, per the standing small-targeted-patch convention.

**Not yet done:** hasn't been confirmed fixed live by Lauren yet (this was diagnosed and shipped in the same session as her report, before a live recheck). If she reports it's still happening after this deploys, the next thing to check is whether `cropx-scroll-reveal-safety-net` actually made it into the page's script tags (WP File Manager's history of dropping files on "successful" extracts — see CLAUDE.md) before assuming the fix itself is wrong.

## September 15, 2026 — Repaired the 9 Pages found in the `\` re-scan (unicode-escape corruption)

**Context.** Picking up the item deliberately deferred from earlier today (see the "Re-ran unicode-escape corruption scan" entry below) once the scroll-reveal round 4 rollout was delivered. That scan had found 9 Pages still flagged by `edit.php?s=u005c` (Careers, CropX Acquires EnGeniousAg, CropX Acquires SCIO, FarmLook & APIs, Green Brain Acquisition Australia, Mission & Sustainability, SCIO Material Analysis Cup, SCIO Mini 2 Pocket Spectrometer, and the private Utility Page: Patterns) but left them unrepaired, flagging a decision between rebuilding the deleted admin tool or a manual fix.

**Approach chosen: direct REST API fix, not a rebuilt admin tool.** Followed the Sep 11 precedent (see below) rather than resurrecting `inc/unicode-escape-repair.php` — for a bounded, one-time batch of 9 pages this is faster, and lets every block's JSON be individually validated with `JSON.parse()` before saving rather than trusting a blind regex tool. Logged into wp-admin via the in-app browser (Lauren's own staging session), pulled the WP REST nonce, and worked directly against `/wp-json/wp/v2/pages/<id>` with `context=edit` to get raw, unfiltered `post_content`.

**Root-cause nuance found that the Sep 11 fix didn't need to handle.** The established comment-aware method (restore the missing backslash on a bare `u003c`/`u003e`/`u0026`/`u0022` token inside a block's JSON attribute span, since that's what `wp_unslash()` originally stripped) turned out to be incomplete for these 9 pages: they also carried bare `u005c` tokens, and naively restoring those to `\` (a real backslash character) produced broken output — e.g. the Careers page's "CropX CEO AMA" quote decoded to `\<p\>Hello everyone,\<` with a stray backslash sitting inside every tag, which the browser (and `wp_kses_post()`, which parses tags the same tag-boundary-first way) then failed to recognize as real `<p>` tags at all. Traced this empirically (decoded a token-by-token character dump, then confirmed against a totally different page's `&amp;` corruption) and found the pattern is 100% consistent across all 9 pages: every bare `u005c` sits immediately before an already-otherwise-correctly-escaped `&`/`<`/`>` token, meaning it's residue from an earlier (already-applied) repair pass compounding with a later corruption incident — not a legitimate escaped backslash in the original content. The correct fix is to delete bare `u005c` outright rather than re-escape it. Also had to reorder the existing tag-boundary `nn`→`\n\n` fix to run *after* the `u005c` deletion (a leftover `u005c` was sitting directly between the `nn` run and its `u003c`/`u003e` anchor, blocking that regex from matching).

**Verification before touching anything live:** ran the repair against all 9 pages' fetched raw content first, checked `JSON.parse()` succeeded on every block's attribute span both before and after the fix (zero failures), decoded every span back out and scanned every string value for leftover tokens, stray backslash-adjacent-to-tag-character patterns, and literal "nn" — zero anomalies found across all 9 pages. Only then `POST`ed the fixed content back per page via the REST API (which sidesteps `wp_update_post()`'s `wp_slash()` bug entirely, same as the Sep 11 fix), re-fetched each afterward to confirm the save round-tripped cleanly, and re-ran the `edit.php?s=u005c` search sitewide — "No pages found."

**Spot-checked live:** `/about/careers/` — the CEO AMA quote now renders as 7 clean `<p>` paragraphs with real line breaks, no stray text. `/nitrogen-sensing/` (CropX Acquires EnGeniousAg) — the "Contact us" link is now a real, working anchor tag (its `href` still points at the old EC2 domain, a separate pre-existing issue unrelated to this corruption, out of scope here).

**Not a theme/build change** — this was a content-only fix applied directly to the 9 posts' `post_content` via the REST API, no `functions.php`/block files touched, no version bump, no deploy zip needed.

## September 15, 2026 — Scroll-reveal round 4: Mid-page CTA, Pre-footer CTA, Newsletter CTA, 2-Column Animated, 4 form blocks, App Badges — build propagated + packaged

**Context.** The scroll-reveal-on-scroll animation was already wired at the **source** level (`src/blocks/<name>/`) for 9 blocks in an earlier pass — this session's job was purely to propagate those already-correct source edits into the **compiled** `build/blocks/<name>/` folders that the live site actually serves (`npm run build` is broken in this sandbox, so `src/` → `build/` propagation is done by hand: copy `render.php` byte-verbatim, hand-patch the minified CSS/JS, create `view.asset.php` where a block is getting its first-ever `viewScript`).

**The 9 blocks, each block's observed root selector, and single-instance (`reveal-up`) vs. repeated-item (`reveal-group`/`reveal-item`) pattern:**
- `mid-page-cta` (`.cropx-mid-cta`) — single-instance `reveal-up` on eyebrow/heading/body/actions. Done as the worked example before this session started; verified consistent with the other 8.
- `pre-footer-cta` (`.pf`) — single-instance `reveal-up`.
- `newsletter-cta` (`.ncta-section`) — single-instance `reveal-up`.
- `two-column-animated` (`.tcap-section`) — single-instance `reveal-up`, **text column only** (see special case below).
- `contact-form` (`.cf-section`) — single-instance `reveal-up` on the intro column + form card.
- `zoho-contact-form` (`.zcf-section`) — single-instance `reveal-up` on the intro column + form card.
- `zoho-form` (`.zform-section`) — single-instance `reveal-up` on the header + form card.
- `split-contact-columns` (`.scc-section`) — repeated-item pattern: header gets `reveal-up`, each contact column gets `reveal-item` inside a `reveal-group`.
- `app-badges` (`.app-badges`) — repeated-item pattern: each badge gets `reveal-item` inside a `reveal-group`.

**Two-Column Animated special case.** Only the `.tcap-content` text column gets `reveal-up` — the `.tcap-visual` photo/overlay column is deliberately left untouched. That column already runs its own continuous CSS-only crossfade/rotation animation between image pairs (see its `render.php`/`style.css`), so adding a one-time scroll-triggered reveal on top would fight that ongoing animation (a competing opacity/transform state machine on the same element) rather than complement it — the visual column doesn't need a reveal because it's never static to begin with.

**Build propagation done for all 8 remaining blocks (mirroring the `mid-page-cta` reference pattern):**
- `render.php` copied byte-verbatim from `src/` to `build/` for all 8 — confirmed identical via `diff`.
- The canonical minified scroll-reveal CSS block prepended to both `style-index.css` and `style-index-rtl.css` for all 8 (identical text, no RTL-sensitive properties involved).
- The per-block settled-state editor override (`.wp-block-cropx-<name> .reveal-up{opacity:1!important;...}`, plus `.reveal-item` in the selector list for `split-contact-columns` and `app-badges`) appended to each block's existing `index.css`/`index-rtl.css` (all 8 already had these files from prior rounds — none needed creating).
- `view.js` compiled per block: `pre-footer-cta`, `newsletter-cta`, `two-column-animated`, `split-contact-columns`, `app-badges` got the standalone minified scrollReveal IIFE (selector substituted in, template matches `build/blocks/mid-page-cta/view.js`). `contact-form`, `zoho-contact-form`, `zoho-form` already had a `view.js` for form-submission handling — the existing minified logic was left untouched and the scrollReveal IIFE was appended directly after it (two back-to-back `(()=>{...})();` statements, same concatenation style as `build/blocks/video/view.js`).
- `view.asset.php` created (`<?php return array('dependencies' => array(), 'version' => '1');`) for the 5 blocks that didn't have one yet: `pre-footer-cta`, `newsletter-cta`, `two-column-animated`, `split-contact-columns`, `app-badges`. The 3 form blocks already had one from before and were left as-is.
- `block.json` updated with `"viewScript": "file:./view.js"` for the 5 blocks above (inserted in the same position as `src/blocks/<name>/block.json` — immediately before `"render"` for `pre-footer-cta`/`newsletter-cta`/`two-column-animated`/`app-badges`, and immediately after `"style"` for `split-contact-columns`, whose `"render"` key happens to sit earlier in that block's key order). `contact-form`, `zoho-contact-form`, `zoho-form` already had `viewScript` in their build `block.json` and were left untouched.

**Verification — all passed:** JSON validity on all 9 `block.json` files; `{`/`}` brace balance on every touched `style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css`/`index.css`/`index-rtl.css` across all 9 blocks; `node --check` on all 9 `view.js` files; `{`/`}` and `(`/`)` balance check on all 9 `render.php` files; byte-identical `diff` of `render.php` between `src/` and `build/` for all 8 newly-propagated blocks (plus a re-check of `mid-page-cta`). Nothing failed on the first pass.

**Version bump:** `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.72` → `0.4.73` in `functions.php` (required any time a block's compiled CSS/JS changes, per the cache-busting convention documented in `CLAUDE.md`).

**Shipped as** `/tmp/scroll-reveal-round4-deploy.zip` (all 9 blocks' current post-edit `build/blocks/<name>/` folders, structured as `blocks/<name>/...` for direct extraction into `wp-content/themes/cropx/build/blocks/` via WP File Manager) / `/tmp/scroll-reveal-round4-revert.zip` (same 9 block folders restored to their pre-this-round originals, from the snapshot taken before any edits). `functions.php` was **not** included in either zip (a full-file WP File Manager patch is riskier than these block folders per `CLAUDE.md`'s deploy guidance) — the version bump is committed in the repo only; Lauren needs to apply the one-line `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` change to the live `functions.php` separately, or confirm it's covered by a different deploy.

## September 15, 2026 — Re-ran unicode-escape corruption scan: found 8 more affected Pages (not yet repaired)

**Trigger.** Lauren spotted `u005c&` on the live About > Sustainability page and asked to re-run the corruption scan (same bug family as the Sep 10/11/14 incidents — see the `wp_slash()` root-cause entry below). The standing Unicode Escape Repair tool no longer exists (removed Sep 2026 once the earlier known incidents were believed resolved), so this round was done by hand via wp-admin search + live front-end inspection (browser-based; no server file/DB access in this session).

**Method.** WP admin's `edit.php?s=u005c` search (post title/content/excerpt LIKE match) was run across all 8 content post types (Posts, Pages, Patterns/`wp_block`, Results & Research, Team Members, Resources, Dealers, Quotes). Searching for the bare `u005c` token (the escaped-backslash artifact) rather than `u003c`/`u0026` directly, since **any** backslash-escaped character corrupted by the `wp_unslash()` double-strip bug leaves this same tell, regardless of which character was originally escaped — `u003c`/`u0026` substring search is unreliable on its own because legitimately-escaped HTML inside RichText JSON attributes also contains those exact substrings (just correctly backslash-prefixed), so it flags far too many false positives.

**Result: 9 Pages flagged, 8 confirmed corrupted on the live front-end** (checked via `get_page_text` on each): 
- `/about/sustainability/` — "u005c&" in 4 headings (Lauren's original report)
- `/about/careers/` — worst case found: the "CropX CEO AMA" quote block lost **all** its paragraph breaks and quotation marks (`u005cHello everyone,u005cnnu005cThank you...Tomer Tzachu005cCropX CEOu005c`), plus the shared "People, Planet & Prosperity" pre-footer CTA blurb also shows `u005c&`
- `/why-cropx-acquired-scio/` — `u005c&amp;` in an H2
- `/nitrogen-sensing/` (CropX Acquires EnGeniousAg) — a "Contact us" **link's markup is destroyed** (`u005cu005cu005cContact usu005c to learn more...`), not just cosmetic — the hyperlink likely no longer works
- `/cropx-platform/farmlook/` — 5+ instances of `u005c&`, including in the page title itself
- `/cropx-acquires-green-brain-australia/` — a `<strong>` tag and a link both destroyed the same way as the EnGeniousAg page
- `/cropx-platform/scio-material-analysis-cup/` — 2 instances of `u005c&`
- `/cropx-platform/scio-mini-pocket-spectrometer/` — 2 instances of `u005c&`
- `Utility Page: Patterns` (private page) — flagged by the search but **not checked** on the front end (private/internal, not public-facing)

No corruption found in Posts, Patterns, Results & Research, Team Members, Resources, Dealers, or Quotes.

**Is this new, or old damage that was never repaired?** Unclear from the browser alone — no server file access this session to check whether `wpslash-fix-deploy.zip` (packaged Sep 15, see entry below) actually made it to staging. What's fairly clear: these are likely **pre-existing, un-repaired corruption**, not a fresh recurrence — all 8 pages were last touched (per the admin list's dates) in late July/August, before the root cause was even diagnosed (Sep 15), and every prior repair pass targeted specific known-incident batches rather than a full sitewide sweep, so these acquisition/product pages plausibly fell outside all of them.

**Not yet fixed.** This was scan-only. Repairing these 8 (9th unchecked) pages needs either: (a) manually re-typing the affected text/links in the block editor, or (b) rebuilding a repair admin tool (the last one was deleted) using the corrected backslash-restore method, with `wp_slash()` now fixed. Flagging for Lauren to decide which approach before proceeding.

## September 15, 2026 — Scroll-reveal on Logo Carousel/3-Column Segment Nav/UN Goals Nav/Video Showcase + mobile mega-menu Hardware cutoff fix

**Scroll-reveal round 5.** Lauren asked to continue the scroll-reveal rollout ("Let's do the Logo Carousel as well as the 3-Column Segment Navigation, UN Sustainability Goals Nav, and 1- or 2-column Video Showcase"). Wired the shared `initScrollReveal()` utility (`src/shared/scrollReveal.js`) into all 4:

- **Logo Carousel (`logo-strip`):** `.logo-strip` observed root; eyebrow + the whole marquee wrapper each get `reveal-up` (0.05s / 0.15s delays) as two independent elements under one `reveal-group` — no per-logo `reveal-item` staggering, since the marquee already renders every logo twice for its seamless-loop animation and per-logo stagger would double up and fight that motion.
- **3-Column Segment Navigation (`segments`):** `.seg-section` observed root; header eyebrow/heading get `reveal-up` (0.05s/0.15s); each of the 3 panels gets `reveal-item` with the standard `0.3 + (min(index,8) * 0.06)`s stagger.
- **UN Sustainability Goals Nav (`un-goals-nav`):** `.usn-section` observed root; intro eyebrow/heading/body get `reveal-up` (0.05s/0.15s/0.25s); each grid item gets `reveal-item` with the standard stagger formula. This block had **no `view.js`/`viewScript` at all** before this round — both were created from scratch (see revert note below).
- **Video Showcase (`video`):** `.cropx-video` observed root; eyebrow/heading/body get `reveal-up` (0.05s/0.15s/0.25s); each video item gets `reveal-item` with the standard stagger, reusing the loop's existing `$index` variable.

All 4 follow the established conventions: canonical scroll-reveal CSS block copy-pasted into each block's `style.css` (webpack can't resolve a cross-folder `@import` in this build), settled-state `.reveal-up{opacity:1!important;...}` override in each `editor.css` so the editor canvas never shows the hidden pre-reveal state, and bare semantic-class selectors (not `.wp-block-cropx-*`) per the safety-first habit established during the two-column round (none of these 4 blocks actually have the `className:false` gotcha, but using the bare class costs nothing and stays consistent).

**Mobile mega-menu bug fix (unrelated, bundled into the same patch).** Lauren reported the hamburger/mega-menu's Hardware section cut off right after "Rivo Rain Gauge" with no way to scroll to the rest, at every viewport width the hamburger menu shows. Root cause: `src/blocks/nav/style.css`'s `.cnav-mobile-item.is-open .cnav-mobile-sub` accordion rule had a hardcoded `max-height: 700px` — fine for the original short dropdowns, but the Platform item's mobile accordion flattens its *entire* mega-menu (View all products link + full Hardware list + full Software list) into that one `.cnav-mobile-sub` div, which runs well past 700px. Past the cap, `overflow: hidden` silently clipped everything else with no way to reach it (the outer `.cnav-mobile-panel`'s own `overflow-y: auto` is a sibling concern and doesn't help — the clip happens one level in). Fix: raised the cap to `max-height: 3000px`, comfortably covering the combined Hardware+Software list with headroom for future additions.

**Build propagation:** `render.php` copied verbatim into `build/` for all 4 scroll-reveal blocks (byte-identical, confirmed); compiled `view.js`/`style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css`/`index.css`/`index-rtl.css` hand-patched (minified reveal-init IIFE + canonical CSS + override CSS inserted by hand, matching the established pattern from every prior scroll-reveal round); `un-goals-nav/block.json` updated with `"viewScript": "file:./view.js"` (both `src/` and `build/`) and a brand-new `build/blocks/un-goals-nav/view.js` + `view.asset.php` created (this block had neither before). Nav block's `style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css` patched with the `700px` → `3000px` swap. Verified: PHP brace/paren balance, JSON validity on every touched `block.json`, `node --check` on every touched JS file, CSS brace-balance on every touched stylesheet — all clean.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.71` → `0.4.72`.

**Shipped as** `scroll-reveal-and-nav-fix-deploy.zip` (5 block folders — `logo-strip`, `segments`, `un-goals-nav`, `video`, `nav` — plus `functions.php`) / `scroll-reveal-and-nav-fix-revert.zip` (same 5 block folders restored to their pre-this-round state + `functions.php` reverted to `0.4.71`). **Revert note:** `un-goals-nav`'s revert folder deliberately does **not** include `view.js` or `view.asset.php` — those files didn't exist before this round, so a true revert means deleting them, not overwriting them. If rolling back, extract the revert zip's `un-goals-nav` folder over the live one, then manually delete `wp-content/themes/cropx/build/blocks/un-goals-nav/view.js` and `view.asset.php` via WP File Manager (the revert zip alone won't remove files that a plain overwrite-extract can't delete — same caveat as every prior round that introduced a first-time `view.js` for a previously-view.js-less block, e.g. Two-Column and Two-Column-with-Overlay).

**Status: packaged, not yet deployed to staging** — Lauren needs to upload+extract `scroll-reveal-and-nav-fix-deploy.zip` via WP File Manager (small targeted patch: unzip into `wp-content/themes/cropx/` so `blocks/` lands under `build/blocks/` and `functions.php` lands at the theme root), then hard-refresh and check: the 4 blocks reveal on scroll, and the mobile hamburger menu's Hardware list is fully visible/scrollable at multiple widths.
