## September 15, 2026 — Removed "Migrate White Backgrounds" and "CropX System → Platform Rename" admin tools

Both were one-time content-sweep tools that had already fully served their purpose (all white-background content migrated to Taupe/Warm White; all "CropX System" wording renamed to "CropX Platform" sitewide — see their original PROGRESS.md entries below for history). Lauren asked to remove them now that they're done. Removed at Lauren's request, not because of the `wp_slash()` bug above — both tools were already patched with that fix earlier today and were safe to keep running; they're just no longer needed.

**Done:** deleted `inc/bgcolor-white-migration.php` and `inc/cropx-system-to-platform-rename.php` from the theme, and removed their guarded `require_once` blocks from `functions.php` (replaced with a short note, matching the existing convention already used for the Media URL Cleanup / Zoho Form URL Fix / Unicode Escape Repair tools removed earlier this month). Their "Tools" menu entries (Tools → Migrate White Backgrounds, Tools → CropX System → Platform Rename) will disappear from wp-admin once this ships. The Self-Replace Corruption Test tool (Tools → CropX Diagnostics) is untouched — still active while Lauren verifies the `wp_slash()` fix.

**Shipped as** `remove-admin-tools-deploy.zip` (updated `functions.php` only) / `remove-admin-tools-revert.zip` (original `functions.php` with both guarded requires restored + both deleted `inc/` files, wp_slash()-fixed versions, in case either tool needs to come back). To deploy: upload+extract `functions.php` over the theme root via WP File Manager as usual, then separately select and delete `inc/bgcolor-white-migration.php` and `inc/cropx-system-to-platform-rename.php` in File Manager's file browser (no upload needed for a deletion — functions.php's guarded `file_exists()` check means the files being merely present-but-unrequired is already harmless, but removing them fully is tidier). No `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bump needed — PHP-only, no front-end asset caching involved.

## September 15, 2026 — Diagnostic tool: deliberate self-replace corruption repro test (Tools → CropX Diagnostics)

**Why.** Lauren asked to deliberately run a search/replace "similar to the ones that seem to have caused" the recurring unicode-escape corruption (Sep 10/11/14 incidents — bare `u003c`/`u003e`/`u0026` tokens and stray "n"/"nn" runs where escaped `\n` should be), to help pin down the actual trigger. TranslatePress was identified as the likely source on Sep 11, but the corruption recurred Sep 14 anyway, so that's not fully confirmed.

**Design.** Rather than a real content change, the tool searches for the word "aggregates" and replaces it with **itself** — byte-for-byte identical text — then saves via `wp_update_post()`, the exact same save path used by the CropX System → Platform rename tool and the bgColor migration tool. A no-op replacement isolates one variable: does simply round-tripping content through this save pipeline reproduce the corruption, independent of TranslatePress or of what the replacement text actually is?

Found a live target via site search: the homepage (`new.cropx.com`) has "The CropX platform **aggregates** data from across your entire operation..." in the "One platform for every layer of your operation" section.

**Built:** `wp-theme/cropx/inc/self-replace-diagnostic-test.php`, wired into `functions.php` via the same guarded-`file_exists()`-require pattern as the other one-time admin tools. Tools → CropX Diagnostics → Self-Replace Corruption Test. Scan finds every post containing "aggregates" with context; each row has its own "Run test" button (no bulk-apply — deliberately one post at a time, since this is a live experiment, not a fix). Running the test:
1. Self-replaces the word (no-op) and saves via `wp_update_post()` (creates a normal revision automatically, so anything broken is recoverable the same way the Sep 10 incident was).
2. Re-fetches the saved content fresh from the DB (not the in-memory string) and runs a corruption scan for both known signatures — bare `u00XX` tokens missing their backslash, and `u003e(n+)u003c` newline-run corruption — plus validates every `cropx/*` block's JSON attributes still parse.
3. Reports before/after byte length, whether content came back byte-identical, and clearly flags whether corruption was newly introduced by this test vs. pre-existing vs. absent.

**Shipped as** `srdt-deploy.zip` (functions.php + new inc file — no `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bump needed, PHP-only, no front-end asset caching involved) / `srdt-revert.zip` (functions.php with the require removed + instructions). This is a **temporary tool** — remove once the test has served its purpose.

**RESULT — reproduced, but the TranslatePress theory below (originally written right after this test) was WRONG.** Lauren ran the test on the homepage. It reproduced the corruption immediately and fully: `The u003cemu003ecompleteu003c/emu003e digital agronomy platform`, `ADVISORY U0026 DEALER PLATFORM`, and multiple fully-mangled anchor tags like `u003ca href=u0022http://ec2-100-25-145-190.compute-1.amazonaws.com/products/field-data-management/u0022 ... u003eField Data Managementu003c/au003e` (confirmed live via direct page fetch of `new.cropx.com/` immediately after the test ran). That much was correct — it proved the corruption fires on **any** `wp_update_post()` call, with zero TranslatePress UI/API involvement.

Where this went wrong: the reasoning below blamed TranslatePress for hooking into the save regardless of its "Manual Translation Only" setting. Lauren then checked and found **TranslatePress was already deactivated** (both "TranslatePress – Business" and "TranslatePress – Multilingual" showed "Activate | Delete" in Plugins) — so it couldn't have been involved in this test run at all. That sent the investigation back to first principles.

**ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE (confirmed):** `wp_update_post()` — and `wp_insert_post()` under it — hands its final `$postarr` straight to `$wpdb->update()`/`$wpdb->insert()`, and those functions **unconditionally run `wp_unslash()`** on every field value before writing to the DB (the same convention `$_POST` data follows, since that's normally where this data comes from). Our three admin tools (this diagnostic, the bgColor migration tool, and the CropX System → Platform rename tool) all called `wp_update_post()` with a plain, already-clean `post_content` string pulled from `get_post()` — never slashed. That unslash-on-write step doesn't know the content is "clean, not `$_POST`-sourced" — it just strips one layer of backslashes from everything, including the *legitimate* single backslashes inside a Gutenberg block's JSON-escaped comment attributes (`<`, `&`, `\n`). Strip that backslash and `<` becomes the literal, broken text `u003c` — exactly the corruption signature seen in every incident since Sep 10. Standard WordPress core fix: always `wp_slash()` content before passing it to `wp_update_post()`/`wp_insert_post()` programmatically. All three tools have been patched this way (Sep 15) — see the deploy/revert zips below.

The mangled `ec2-100-25-145-190.compute-1.amazonaws.com` hrefs are unrelated to this bug — that's simply the site's current `home_url()` pending the "put Cloudflare in front of the site" task (#308), and has nothing to do with TranslatePress or the slashing bug.

**TranslatePress is not implicated in any of this** and can be safely reactivated if Lauren wants the language-switcher functionality back — its deactivation was an unnecessary (but harmless) precaution taken before the real cause was found.

**Fix shipped:** `wpslash-fix-deploy.zip` (the 3 patched files: `inc/self-replace-diagnostic-test.php`, `inc/bgcolor-white-migration.php`, `inc/cropx-system-to-platform-rename.php` — each now wraps `post_content` in `wp_slash()` before calling `wp_update_post()`) / `wpslash-fix-revert.zip` (pre-fix versions of the same 3 files, for rollback). No `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bump needed — PHP-only, no front-end asset caching involved.

**Not yet done:** the homepage was left corrupted after this test ran and has not yet been confirmed recovered. Since WordPress's own revision-restore UI (Pages → Home → Edit → Revisions → Restore) handles its own slashing correctly via `wp_restore_post_revision()` (unlike our custom tools, which had the bug above), restoring the pre-test revision through that standard UI should be safe to do directly — no need to touch TranslatePress either way. Confirm the homepage is clean after restoring. Also worth a sitewide re-scan: `bgcolor-white-migration.php` and `cropx-system-to-platform-rename.php` both carried this same bug and were actually run earlier in the project (see their PROGRESS.md entries below) — they may have silently corrupted other pages whenever they made a change, not just during this test.

## September 14, 2026 — URGENT fix: Product Grid broken live after the shared.css "dead code" removal above — it wasn't dead code

**Why.** Immediately after the previous fix (below) shipped, Lauren reported Product Grid visibly broken on `/solutions/dealers/` and `/cropx-platform/` — background/pattern rendered fine, but the grid collapsed into unstyled, full-width stacked items running for tens of thousands of pixels down the page.

**Root cause — I was wrong about the previous fix.** The ~3.5KB `.pg-*` chunk I deleted from `styles/shared.css` was NOT a stale duplicate of `build/blocks/product-grid/style-index.css`. I only checked that the *background-color* rule (`.pg-block--bg-deep-blue`) was duplicated and stale (true — it still had the old pre-Deep-Blue-fix color). I never checked whether the *other* ~28 selectors in that same chunk existed anywhere else. They didn't: `.pg-grid`, `.pg-item`, `.pg-thumb`, `.pg-thumb-img`, `.pg-overlay--card-bleed` and friends, `.pg-text`, `.pg-name`, `.pg-arrow`, `.pg-desc` — the entire card-grid layout — has apparently never lived in the block's own compiled CSS; `shared.css` was its only source. Deleting the "stale duplicate" chunk silently deleted the only copy of the grid layout, so every Product Grid instance on the live site rendered as unstyled stacked `<a>` cards (confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` showing one block ~25,700px tall — 13 uncompressed card images stacked full-width instead of a 3-column grid).

**Diagnosis path (for next time).** The `<link>`-tag stylesheet check I ran first was a false lead — WordPress inlines small per-block stylesheets as `<style>` tags rather than `<link>` tags once they're under its size threshold, so "no product-grid `<link>` found" didn't mean "no CSS loaded." The real tell was `.pg-block`'s rendered height being tens of thousands of pixels — a sign the grid/flex layout wasn't applying at all, not that a stylesheet was missing outright. Compared `build/blocks/product-grid/style-index.css` (the compiled per-block file, confirmed via direct fetch to be genuinely deployed and correct) against the deleted `shared.css` chunk (recovered from the `shared-css-fix-revert.zip` I'd made as a safety net) and found zero overlap in selectors between the two — proof the deleted CSS was live, not dead.

**Fix.** Restored the full deleted chunk to `styles/shared.css`, with only the one genuinely-stale line corrected in place (`​.pg-block--bg-deep-blue{background:var(--deep-blue)}` → `var(--deep-blue-dark)`, matching today's earlier Deep Blue fix) — everything else (grid, cards, thumbnails, overlays, text, arrow, responsive breakpoints) restored byte-for-byte. `shared.css`: 4,369 → 7,953 bytes (vs. the original 7,948 — the 5-byte difference is exactly the `-dark` suffix). Brace-balance verified (85 open / 85 close). Confirmed only 1 occurrence of the new `--deep-blue-dark` token and the other 4 pre-existing `--deep-blue` (non-dark) uses — `.pg-name` text color, `.pg-arrow` icon color, the shared list-marker custom property — are untouched, as they should be per Lauren's original instruction to leave buttons/text on primary Deep Blue.

**Follow-up worth doing (not urgent):** these `.pg-*` rules really belong in `product-grid`'s own `src/style.css` / compiled `build/blocks/product-grid/style-index.css`, not in the global `shared.css` — having a block's entire layout live in a "shared utilities" file is exactly the kind of thing that caused this whole incident. Left in `shared.css` for now to ship the live-site fix fast; migrating it properly is a separate, lower-risk task for later.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.70` → `0.4.71`. Deploy zip: `pg-fix-deploy.zip` (3 files — `functions.php` + `styles/shared.css`). Revert zip: `pg-fix-revert.zip` (4 files + `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt`) — restores `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.70` and `shared.css` to the broken (missing-grid-CSS) state this patch fixes; note this is a revert of *this specific patch*, not a restore of the pre-incident original file.

**Status: fix built and packaged, not yet verified live** (Lauren needs to deploy `pg-fix-deploy.zip` via WP File Manager first) — verify `/solutions/dealers/` and `/cropx-platform/` render the Product Grid as a proper 3-column card layout after deploying, before closing this out.

## September 14, 2026 — Fix: stale Product Grid CSS duplicate in shared.css was overriding the Deep Blue fix

**Why.** Lauren checked DevTools on a page with the Product Grid block (`/solutions/dealers/`) and found the new Deep Blue color was NOT applying there — `.pg-block--bg-deep-blue` from `styles/shared.css` (`background: var(--deep-blue)`, the OLD color) was winning the cascade over the correct, already-fixed rule in `build/blocks/product-grid/style-index.css` (`background: var(--deep-blue-dark)`), shown struck through in her screenshot.

**Root cause.** `styles/shared.css` is one of 3 global stylesheets loaded on every page (`inc/enqueue.php`, alongside `tokens.css`/`content.css`). It contained an entire stale, out-of-date duplicate copy of Product Grid's CSS — ~3.5KB, ~29 selectors (`.pg-block`, `.pg-inner`, `.pg-item`, `.pg-thumb`, etc.) — left over from some earlier point in the block's history. Signs of its age: it still had a `.pg-block--bg-white` rule (the "white" background option was removed sitewide back in an earlier round the same day), `.pg-blurb` instead of the current `.pg-body` class name, and zero scroll-reveal classes (`reveal-group`/`reveal-item`/`reveal-up`) despite Product Grid having been wired for scroll-reveal days ago. Because `.pg-block--bg-deep-blue` has identical CSS specificity in both files, whichever stylesheet's `<link>` prints later in the page wins — and `shared.css` happened to win, silently shadowing every change made to Product Grid's own background rule since this duplicate was created, including today's Deep Blue fix.

**Investigation.** Programmatically searched `shared.css` against all 43 Deep-Blue-background selectors from the earlier fix, and separately against every other block's CSS class prefix (`crd-`, `tci-`, `hwf-`, `fph-`, etc.) — confirmed this stale duplication is isolated to Product Grid only. No other block has a competing copy of its background/section CSS living in `shared.css`.

**Fix.** Removed the entire dead `.pg-*` chunk from `styles/shared.css` (7,948 → 4,369 bytes) rather than just patching the one color value — the block's own `build/blocks/product-grid/style-index.css` is already complete and correct on its own, so the shared.css copy was pure duplication with no purpose except to occasionally (and silently) shadow real fixes. This is the same class of bug as the Aug 2026 "block CSS bloat" investigation, just living in a global file instead of a per-block one.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.69` → `0.4.70`. Deploy zip: `shared-css-fix-deploy.zip` (3 files — `functions.php` + `styles/shared.css`). Revert zip: `shared-css-fix-revert.zip` (4 files + `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt`) — restores the stale chunk byte-for-byte and `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.69`, for symmetry with every other patch (there's no real reason to want the old duplicate back).

## September 14, 2026 — Deep Blue: bump topo pattern overlay opacity to match darker background

**Why.** Follow-up to the Deep Blue background-color change above. Lauren flagged (via a DevTools screenshot of `.ts-section--blue::before`) that with the section background now darker (`#19274d` vs. the old `#243565`), the faint topo drift-pattern overlay — `filter: invert(1) opacity(...)` — reads as too low-contrast against it, and asked to bump the opacity a little sitewide, wherever a Deep Blue background has this pattern overlay.

**Scope.** Grepped every block's `style.css` for `invert(1) opacity(` — found 42 matches across 42 block folders (every block that has both a Deep Blue background variant *and* a topo pattern overlay; `pricing-tiers` has a Deep Blue background but no pattern overlay, so it was correctly out of scope here even though it was in scope for the background-color change). Two starting values existed: `0.07` for 36 standard blocks, and a slightly more subtle `0.06` for the 6 hero-family blocks (`hero`, `hero-blog`, `hero-curved`, `hero-curved-animated`, `hero-curved-standard`, `segment-hero` — these already have a background photo competing for attention, hence the original lower value).

**Change.** Bumped both proportionally: `0.07` → `0.10` (36 blocks), `0.06` → `0.09` (6 hero-family blocks) — preserves the original relative relationship between the two groups while making the pattern about 40-50% more visible everywhere.

**Method.** Exact literal string replace (`invert(1) opacity(0.07)` → `invert(1) opacity(0.1)`, etc.) across all 42 `src/blocks/*/style.css` files, then the minified equivalent (`invert(1) opacity(.07)` → `invert(1) opacity(.1)`, etc.) across all 84 compiled `build/blocks/*/style-index.css` + `style-index-rtl.css` files — this exact string is unique to the pattern-overlay `filter` declaration, so a literal replace was safe without needing selector-scoping.

**Verification:** brace-balance check on all 42 touched `src/style.css` files (all balanced), confirmed zero remaining `0.07`/`0.06` pattern-opacity instances anywhere in `src/` or `build/`, spot-checked both a `0.1` and a `0.09` result.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.68` → `0.4.69`. Deploy zip: `pattern-opacity-deploy.zip` (447 files — `functions.php` + all 42 blocks' full `build/blocks/` folders). Revert zip: `pattern-opacity-revert.zip` (448 files + `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt`) — restores `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.68` and all 42 pattern-opacity values back to `0.07`/`0.06`, without touching the Deep Blue background-color change from earlier the same day.

## September 14, 2026 — Deep Blue: split "block background" color from "primary" color sitewide

**Why.** Lauren asked to change the "Deep Blue (hover)" token's color to `#19274d`, use that new color for every Deep Blue block/section background sitewide (instead of the primary Deep Blue, `#243565`), while keeping primary Deep Blue for buttons, dark headings, and body text exactly as before.

**Token change.** `--deep-blue-dark` (labeled "Darker shade — hover states" in `tokens.css`) changed from `#1B284C` → `#19274d`, in both `tokens/tokens.css` (repo reference copy) and the actually-served `wp-theme/cropx/styles/tokens.css`. `--deep-blue` (`#243565`, the primary token) was not touched.

**Scope investigation.** Grepped every block's `style.css` for `var(--deep-blue)` (175 raw matches) and manually categorized each one: genuine section/block-wrapper `background`/`background-color` declarations for a "Deep Blue" variant (the ones to change) vs. buttons, headings, body text, borders, shadows, badges, and sub-component (card/tag/checkbox) backgrounds that also happen to use the primary Deep Blue token (leave alone, per Lauren's instruction). Landed on **43 section-level background rules across 43 block folders** — every block in the library that offers a "Deep Blue" background option, including the 4 hero-family blocks (`hero`, `hero-blog`, `hero-curved`, `hero-curved-animated`, `hero-curved-standard`, `segment-hero` — their base/fallback background when no photo is set), `segments` (its only background), and `dealer-finder` (`.df-scheme-dark`, its dark sidebar scheme).

**Deliberately left unchanged** (same token, different context):
- All buttons (`--btn-primary-bg` and every block's own button rules) — still `var(--deep-blue)`.
- All heading/body text colors, borders, and box-shadows that reference Deep Blue.
- `resource-downloads`' small uppercase badge pill (`background: var(--deep-blue, #243565)`) — a sub-component, not the section background; this was also the one spot in the codebase with a hardcoded hex fallback alongside the var().
- `segments`' per-segment photo-tint overlay (`color-mix(in srgb, var(--muted-gold) 18%, var(--deep-blue))` etc.) — a decorative tint mixed with the segment accent color, not a flat section background.
- `dealer-finder`'s `--df-primary: var(--deep-blue)` — an accent token used for small UI elements (search button, active pin, text/icons/borders), not the sidebar's own background (which is the separate `.df-scheme-dark` rule, correctly changed).

**Method.** Wrote a Python script that, for each of the 43 (folder, selector) pairs, located the exact CSS rule by selector text, scoped the replacement to only that rule's declaration block (selector-to-closing-brace), and swapped `var(--deep-blue)` → `var(--deep-blue-dark)` only within that scope — never a blind file-wide find/replace, since most files also have unrelated `var(--deep-blue)` uses (buttons, text) in the same file. Ran once against all 43 `src/blocks/*/style.css` files, then again against the compiled `build/blocks/*/style-index.css` and `style-index-rtl.css` (86 files, minified — same selector-scoped approach adapted for single-line CSS). `logo-strip` had 2 replacements per file (the section background itself, plus `--ls-fade-color` which feeds its edge-fade gradients and marquee blend backdrop — both live in the same rule block, so both needed to shift together to stay visually consistent).

**Side effect (expected, not a bug):** any button `:hover` state that already referenced `--deep-blue-dark` for its hover shade (several blocks do, via `--btn-primary-bg-hover`) now resolves to the new, very slightly darker `#19274d` instead of `#1B284C` — a near-imperceptible shift, and exactly what "change the Deep Blue (hover) color" means; button base fills are untouched.

**Verification:** brace-balance check on all 43 touched `src/style.css` files (all balanced), spot-checked that `--btn-primary-bg` and every reviewed heading/text rule still reads `var(--deep-blue)` (not `-dark`), confirmed the 4 "leave alone" cases above are untouched, confirmed both `tokens.css` copies show the new hex.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.67` → `0.4.68`. Deploy zip: `deepblue-bg-deploy.zip` (460 files — `functions.php`, `styles/tokens.css`, all 43 blocks' full `build/blocks/` folders). Revert zip: `deepblue-bg-revert.zip` (461 files + `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt`) — restores `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.67`, `--deep-blue-dark` to `#1B284C`, and all 43 background rules back to `var(--deep-blue)`.

## September 14, 2026 — Scroll-reveal animation: Product & Feature showcase round — Product Grid, Product Tabs, Product Sections, Feature + Stat Card, Hardware Lineup, Field Photos Gallery

**Why.** Continuing the scroll-reveal rollout (rounds 1–4 + two-column family above) into the "Product & feature showcase" group Lauren selected: Product Grid, Product Tabs, Product Sections (jump-nav), Feature + Stat Card, Hardware Lineup, Field Photos Gallery. Same shared system as every prior round — `src/shared/scrollReveal.js`'s `initScrollReveal(selector)`, canonical CSS copy-pasted into each block's `style.css` (webpack can't resolve a cross-folder CSS import), staggered per-item delays via `0.3 + (min(idx,8) * 0.06)`.

**Two architecturally different reveal-group patterns, chosen per block:**
- **Product Tabs** — ONE shared `reveal-group` across both hidden/visible tab panels (`.ptabs-block`), since only one panel is ever visible at a time.
- **Product Sections** — TWO independent `reveal-group`s, one per `.psec-section`, since both sections are simultaneously visible/scrollable on the page — `initScrollReveal('.psec-section')`, not `.psec-block`. The shared `scrollReveal.js` observes every element matching the selector independently, so this "just works" without any change to the shared utility itself.
- Product Grid, Feature + Stat Card, Hardware Lineup, Field Photos all use the simpler single-reveal-group-per-block pattern.

**Field Photos gotcha (recurring):** this block has `"className": false` / `"customClassName": false` in `block.json` (same as Resource Downloads/Photo Grid, fixed Sep 14 earlier today) — `view.js` observes the bare `.fph-section` class, not `.wp-block-cropx-field-photos`, per the now-standing precaution.

**Bug caught before shipping — duplicate `style` attribute (Field Photos `render.php`).** Adding `reveal-up` + an inline `--reveal-delay` style to the eyebrow `<span>` collided with the pre-existing `$eyebrow_color_style` variable, which already outputs its own `style="color:..."` attribute — would have produced invalid HTML with two `style=""` attributes on one element. Caught on review before any build/deploy step; fixed by folding the reveal-delay into `$eyebrow_color_style`'s own two-branch construction instead of adding a second literal `style=` to the markup.

**Bug caught and fixed — Unicode mojibake in Product Grid `render.php`.** A comment's em-dash, corrupted into literal bytes (`â\x80\x94`) by an earlier bash-fallback text edit, was found while diffing files for the revert package. Same corruption class as the sitewide Unicode Escape Repair incident (Sep 10) — fixed with a targeted byte replace in both `src` and `build` copies; confirmed zero mojibake bytes remain in the shipped deploy zip.

**Files changed (×6 blocks: feature-stat, product-grid, product-tabs, product-sections, hardware-lineup, field-photos):** `render.php` — `reveal-group`/`reveal-item`/`reveal-up` classes + `--reveal-delay` inline styles on section wrapper(s), header eyebrow/heading/body, and repeated items (cards, pills, panels, photos). `style.css` — canonical scroll-reveal CSS block copy-pasted in. `view.js` — added `initScrollReveal(...)` call with the correct selector for each block's architecture (new file for feature-stat and product-grid, which had no `view.js` before this round; prepended to the existing `view.js` for the other four). `block.json` — added `"viewScript": "file:./view.js"` for feature-stat and product-grid only (the other four already had it).

Propagated to `build/`: `render.php` copied wholesale from `src/` (confirmed untouched by webpack); minified reveal-init IIFE + canonical CSS block hand-inserted into each block's compiled `view.js`/`style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css`; `block.json` re-serialized with `viewScript` added, matching webpack's own 2-space/no-trailing-newline output format. Verified: PHP brace/paren balance (12 files), JSON validity (4 block.json files touched), `node --check` JS syntax (12 view.js files, src as ES modules via temp `.mjs` copies + build as-is), CSS brace-balance self-consistency (12 style files) — all passed.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.66` → `0.4.67`. Deploy zip: `product-showcase-reveal-deploy.zip` (69 files — `functions.php` + all 6 blocks' full `build/blocks/` folders). Revert zip: `product-showcase-reveal-revert.zip` (66 files + `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt` — the two new `view.js`/`view.asset.php` pairs for feature-stat/product-grid can't be removed by a plain zip overwrite, so the instructions tell Lauren to manually delete those 4 files via WP File Manager for a full revert).

## September 14, 2026 — Logo Carousel: multiply-blend fix v2 — root-caused why v1 had no visible effect

**Why.** Lauren reported the v1 fix (below) "doesn't look like it took" on https://new.cropx.com/. Live DOM inspection confirmed `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` was already `0.4.65` (v1 was live) and `getComputedStyle()` on `.ls-logo` correctly reported `mix-blend-mode: multiply` — so the CSS had deployed and applied exactly as written. But visually the logo tiles were unchanged: still visibly brighter than the Warm White section background.

**Root cause.** `.ls-track` (the scrolling marquee track) has `transform: matrix(...)` applied by its scroll `animation` at all times — any non-`none` `transform` makes an element establish its own CSS stacking context, per spec. `mix-blend-mode` can only blend an element against paint that lives *inside that same stacking context*. `.ls-track` has no background of its own (transparent), so `.ls-logo`'s `multiply` blend was compositing against nothing — a no-op that computes correctly but produces zero visible change — and only *then* does the whole (still-white-looking) track get composited normally onto the real section background one level up. This is why DevTools showed `multiply` applied while the page looked untouched.

**Fix.** Added `background-color: var(--ls-fade-color, var(--taupe-50))` directly to `.ls-track` — this gives `.ls-logo`'s blend mode a real, local, stacking-context-correct backdrop to multiply against (the same color as the section background, so it's visually seamless either way). Also consolidated the `--ls-fade-color` custom property: it's now set once at `.logo-strip--bg-deep-blue` (the actual variant selector) instead of narrowly on `.ls-marquee::before`/`::after`, so it cascades as a real inherited value to every descendant that reads it — both the fade-edge gradients *and* `.ls-track`'s new blend backdrop.

**Files changed:** same three files as v1 — `src/blocks/logo-strip/style.css`, `build/blocks/logo-strip/style-index.css`, `build/blocks/logo-strip/style-index-rtl.css`. `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.65` → `0.4.66`. Deploy zip: `logo-strip-blend-fix-v2-deploy.zip` (14 files). Revert zip: `logo-strip-blend-fix-v2-revert.zip` (15 files, `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS2.txt`) — reverts to the v1-deployed state (0.4.65), not all the way back to pure-white-background behavior.

## September 14, 2026 — Logo Carousel: multiply-blend fix for logo tiles against Warm White (v1 — superseded, see v2 above)

**Why.** Right after the "White" background removal shipped (see below), Lauren flagged that the Logo Carousel's individual logo images now visibly mismatch the new Warm White section background: "See how there's a distinction between the white of the logo image background and the off-white we just created for the background? Maybe the solution is to set the transparency blend for each logo image to multiply, or something like that?" — followed by a screenshot showing each logo (ABInBev, HELM, Pepsico, McCain, Nestlé, Dairy Holdings, Sumitomo, etc.) sitting in a visibly brighter rectangular tile.

**Root cause.** Most uploaded logo files are flattened images with their own white (or near-white) canvas baked in. Against the old pure-white section background that canvas was invisible; against Warm White (`rgb(253,251,247)`) it now reads as a slightly-too-bright rectangle around each logo.

**Fix — `mix-blend-mode: multiply`, CSS-only, no image files touched.** White is a no-op under multiply blend (white × anything = that same anything), so each logo's own white canvas dissolves into whatever's actually behind it, while the logo's real ink/color darkens the background normally — the same effect as ink printed on paper. Scoped to `.logo-strip--bg-taupe .ls-logo` only — multiplying a white-canvas logo against a dark background (Deep Blue) would crush it toward black instead of disappearing, so this is intentionally not applied there. (Deep Blue is dormant for this block today — `render.php`'s whitelist only ever resolves `bgColor` to `'taupe'` — but the CSS is still scoped correctly in case it's re-enabled.)

**Related bug caught in the same pass — marquee fade-edge gradients.** The `.ls-marquee::before`/`::after` edge-fade gradients were hardcoded to `var(--white)`, which would leave a visible pure-white smear at each edge now that the section background is Warm White rather than pure white. Fixed by introducing a `--ls-fade-color` custom property (default `var(--taupe-50)`) that the gradients reference instead — mirrors the existing `--ls-pattern-url` pattern already used for the dormant Deep Blue drift-pattern background. Also added a `.logo-strip--bg-deep-blue` override (`--ls-fade-color: var(--deep-blue)`) for the same future-proofing reason, even though that variant isn't reachable via the editor today.

**Files changed:** `src/blocks/logo-strip/style.css` and the compiled `build/blocks/logo-strip/style-index.css` + `style-index-rtl.css` (hand-patched — sandbox can't run `npm run build`; RTL file's gradient directions are mirrored, not identical strings). No `block.json`/`edit.js`/`render.php` changes needed — `edit.js` only imports `editor.css`, so the `style.css` edit alone reaches both the front-end and the editor preview per the established global-CSS convention (gotcha #3).

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.64` → `0.4.65`. Deploy zip: `logo-strip-multiply-blend-deploy.zip` (14 files — full `build/blocks/logo-strip/` folder + `functions.php`). Revert zip: `logo-strip-multiply-blend-revert.zip` (15 files, includes `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt` — no files were added this round, so revert is a straight overwrite, nothing to manually delete).

## September 14, 2026 — Design Token Playground + sitewide token/background changes

**Why.** Lauren asked for an easy way to experiment with design tokens (colors, typography, spacing/radius) and see the effect across real site components before committing to a change. Built `tokens/design-token-playground.html` — a self-contained interactive tool (mini page mockup: nav, hero, segment icons, feature section, card grid, stats band, form, type specimen, spacing bars) with live sliders/color pickers and a "Copy updated CSS" button. Not deployed to the live site — it's a local sandbox for trying combinations, distinct from the static `tokens/tokens-reference.html` swatch page.

Lauren then used the playground to settle on three concrete sitewide changes and asked to apply them:

**1. Section padding — `--section-py`: 5rem (80px) → 7.25rem (116px).** Curved hero blocks, the site nav, and the footer never reference this token (they use their own custom padding) — confirmed via grep before changing, so no exclusion logic was needed; bumping the token only affects the ~30 blocks that actually use `var(--section-py)`. `--section-py-lg` derives via `calc()` so it auto-updated (8.5rem).

**2. H2 size — `--fs-h2`: `clamp(1.875rem, 3vw, 2.5rem)` → `clamp(1.781rem, 3vw, 2.375rem)`.** Lauren's playground value was a flat 2.200rem, but she asked for something proportional to the H1/H3 scale instead. Today's scale isn't a strict ratio (H1→H2 = 1.70×, H2→H3 = 1.82×, average ~1.76×); 2.375rem (38px) keeps both step ratios close to that average (1.79× and 1.73×) while still visibly tightening H2, vs. 2.200rem which would have skewed the rhythm (1.93×/1.60×). The responsive minimum was scaled down proportionally too (1.875rem→1.781rem, preserving the ~0.75 min/max ratio) rather than just capping the desktop size.

**3. Removed the "White" section-background option sitewide — 36 blocks.** Taupe 50 is renamed "Warm White" in the editor (internal attribute value/slug stays `taupe` — zero migration risk for already-`taupe` content) and recolored from `#fbfaf9` to `rgb(253,251,247)` / `#fdfbf7`. Deep Blue + Topo is unaffected. This was **not** a simple find-replace — investigation turned up 3 different attribute names carrying this option depending on the block family:
  - `bgColor` — 27 blocks (the majority; two-column family, cards, stats-grid, faq-accordion, forms, etc.)
  - `backgroundStyle` — 3 blocks (People Showcase, Mid-page CTA, Video)
  - `backgroundVariant` — 6 blocks (Split/Six/Three-Column Icons, Icon Columns, Testimonial Single, Split Contact Columns) — these already excluded "white" from their PHP whitelist and used it as the implicit *fallback* value instead, so the fix here was redirecting that fallback to `taupe`, not removing a whitelist entry.

  Also had to watch for a trap: several blocks (Cards, Video, People Showcase, Mid-page CTA) have a **separate, unrelated** `eyebrowColor` attribute that also offers a legitimate "White" option (for eyebrow text contrast on Deep Blue sections) — that one was deliberately left untouched everywhere; only the section-background control's own options list was touched, scoped via each file's "Background" label rather than a blind string match.

  Per-block-family changes: `block.json` — removed `"white"` from the enum, changed any `"default": "white"` to `"taupe"`. `edit.js`/compiled `index.js` — removed the "White" option row from the Background `SelectControl` (some blocks reference an inline array literal, others a shared `const BG_OPTIONS = [...]` — both patterns handled), renamed the Taupe label to "Warm White". `render.php` — removed `'white'` from `in_array()` whitelists and changed any `?? 'white'` / invalid-value fallback to `'taupe'`; copied to `build/` (byte-identical convention, verified via diff). Did **not** touch `style.css`'s dead `--bg-white` CSS rules (several are multi-line compound rules affecting icon-color overrides, not just background) — they're provably unreachable now that render.php can never output `"white"`, so left as documented dead code rather than risking a 36-file surgical CSS pass for a purely cosmetic cleanup.

  Built a Python script (`/tmp/bgwork/transform.py`, not committed) to apply this mechanically across all 36 blocks × ~4 files, with a full backup taken first. Verified with a second pass: `json.load()` on every `block.json`, `node --check` on every compiled `index.js`, brace/paren balance on every `render.php` and `edit.js`, src/build `render.php` byte-diff — zero problems found.

  **Migration tool for already-published content:** built `inc/bgcolor-white-migration.php` (Tools → Migrate White Backgrounds), same pattern as the existing CropX System→Platform rename tool — scans all post_content for the literal `"bgColor":"white"` / `"backgroundStyle":"white"` / `"backgroundVariant":"white"` JSON substrings (WordPress's own compact block-attribute encoding) and migrates them to `"taupe"`. Needed because a dynamic block's attributes freeze into post_content as JSON at save time — editing block.json/render.php doesn't reach back into already-published pages. Nothing on the live site can actually render white anymore either way (render.php's fallback already coerces any leftover "white" to taupe at render time) — this tool just cleans up the *saved* value so the editor's Background dropdown shows the correct selection instead of an invalid one. Guarded `require_once` in `functions.php`, same as the other one-time tools.

  `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.63` → `0.4.64`. Deploy zip: `bgcolor-tokens-deploy.zip` (388 files — all 36 blocks' `build/` folders, `functions.php`, `inc/bgcolor-white-migration.php`, `styles/tokens.css`). Revert zip: `bgcolor-tokens-revert.zip` (387 files + `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt` — the new migration-tool file can't be removed by a plain zip overwrite, so the instructions tell Lauren to manually delete `inc/bgcolor-white-migration.php` via WP File Manager for a full revert). `tokens/tokens.css` at the repo root and the deployed `wp-theme/cropx/styles/tokens.css` (minified copy actually served on the site) were both updated — confirmed via `inc/enqueue.php` that the theme serves the `styles/` copy, not the repo-root reference file directly.

## September 14, 2026 — Scroll-reveal animation: two-column family — 2 Columns w/ Text & Photo, Alternating Text & Photos, Text & Product Illustration, Text & Video, UN Goals 2-Column

**Why.** After the round-4 scroll-reveal blocks and the resource-grid selector-bug fix, Lauren asked to tackle the two-column family next: "The two-column family (near-identical structure, same pattern each time): 2 Columns with Text & Photo, 2 Columns with Alternating Text & Photos, 2 Columns with Text & Product Illustration, 2 Columns with Text & Video, and UN Goals - 2 Column Text & Photo." This maps to 5 blocks: `two-column`, `two-column-alternating`, `two-column-overlay`, `two-column-video`, `un-goals-two-column`.

**New standing precaution, applied this round:** before wiring reveal into any block, check its `block.json` `supports` for `className: false` (the bug that broke Resource Downloads/Photo Grid above) and prefer the block's own bare semantic class as the `view.js` observed root over the `.wp-block-cropx-*` prefix, since it's simpler and immune to that failure mode. Verified via `json.load()` that none of these 5 blocks have `className: false` (all have the standard `{"html": false, "align": false, "anchor": true}`), so the bare-class choice below is a safety-first habit, not a required workaround, this time.

**Wiring, per block — two treatments used depending on whether the block repeats:**
- **Two-Column Text & Photo** (`src/blocks/two-column/`), **2 Columns with Text & Product Illustration** (`src/blocks/two-column-overlay/`), **2 Columns with Text & Video** (`src/blocks/two-column-video/`), **UN Goals - 2 Column Text & Photo** (`src/blocks/un-goals-two-column/`): all four are single-instance layouts with no repeated items, so no `reveal-group`/`reveal-item` — `view.js` observes the section itself directly (`.tcv-section`, `.tco-section`, `.tcvid-section`, `.ugp-section`) and each header element gets `reveal-up` with a staggered delay: icon-wrap 0.05s, eyebrow 0.1s, heading 0.2s, body 0.3s, CTA 0.4s, and the visual/video column wrapper 0.15s. UN Goals additionally has an optional "Back to top" link, staggered last at 0.45s, and its CTA can render as either a link or a video-opening `<button>` — all 4 CTA variants got the same 0.4s treatment.
- **2 Columns with Alternating Text & Photos** (`src/blocks/two-column-alternating/`): this one repeats — an optional intro plus one row per photo — so it follows the People Showcase/Resource Downloads per-subsection pattern instead: each `.tca-row` (and the `.tca-intro` block, when shown) is independently observed by `view.js` (`initScrollReveal('.tca-intro, .tca-row')`), and children get plain `reveal-up` (not `reveal-item`) since there's no need for `reveal-group` when each row already is the observed root. Intro heading/body/CTA staggered 0.05s/0.15s/0.25s; within each row, `.tca-content` at 0.05s and `.tca-photo-col` at 0.15s — no running counter across rows, since each row triggers independently as it scrolls into view. This block already had a `view.js` (a height-sync IIFE matching text-column height to photo height) — the new `initScrollReveal()` call was added alongside it, not in place of it.

**Build workaround (npm run build still broken in this sandbox):** copied all 5 updated `render.php` files verbatim into `build/` (verified byte-identical via `diff`). Two-Column and Two-Column-with-Overlay had no `view.js` before this round — created new minified `view.js` + `view.asset.php` for both and added `"viewScript"` to their compiled `block.json`. The other three (Two-Column Alternating, Two-Column Video, UN Goals 2-Column) already had a `view.js` with other front-end logic (height-sync, video lightbox, back-to-top smooth-scroll) — prepended the minified reveal IIFE onto their existing compiled JS rather than replacing it. Appended the canonical scroll-reveal CSS to all 5 blocks' compiled `style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css`, and the settled-state-in-editor-canvas override to their compiled `index.css` only (never `index-rtl.css`, per established precedent). Verified every JS file with `node --check`, every JSON file with `json.load()`, and brace/paren balance on every touched CSS/PHP file — all clean.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.62` → `0.4.63`. Packaged both a forward deploy zip and a revert-backup zip — `two-column-family-scroll-reveal-deploy.zip` / `two-column-family-scroll-reveal-revert.zip` (revert reconstructed from a pre-edit backup of `functions.php` + all 5 blocks' `build/` folders, taken before any edits this round, rather than reverse-engineered after the fact). **Revert note:** Two-Column and Two-Column-with-Overlay had no `view.js` before this round — since a zip extraction can only add/overwrite files, not delete them, the revert zip includes a `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt` calling out that these 2 blocks' new `view.js`/`view.asset.php` (4 files total) need to be deleted by hand in File Manager after extracting. The other three blocks' revert is a plain overwrite with no manual step, since they already had a `view.js` before this round.

**Scroll-reveal is now live on 19 of the site's blocks:** Cards, Testimonials Carousel, Stats Grid, FAQ Accordion, Three-Column Icons, Icon Columns, Resource Downloads, Pricing Tiers, People Showcase, Job Openings, Photo Grid, Testimonial Single, Six-Column Icons, Split-Column Icons, Two-Column Text & Photo, Two-Column Alternating, Two-Column with Overlay, Two-Column Video, UN Goals 2-Column. **Still pending Lauren's go-ahead:** which blocks (if any) to do next — remaining un-animated groups per the last audit were carousels, CTA banners, forms, and product blocks.

## September 14, 2026 — URGENT: Resource Grid content invisible sitewide — scroll-reveal selector bug (Resource Downloads + Photo Grid)

**What Lauren saw.** "Contents of the resource grid block aren't loading at all now." Checked `https://new.cropx.com/knowledge-hub/brochures/` (uses Resource Downloads / "Resource Grid"): the block's text (eyebrow, heading, subheadings, card titles, download buttons) was all present in the DOM — `get_page_text` returned it in full — but a screenshot showed a large blank white gap where the entire block should be. Confirmed via JS console: `.rsd-header.classList.contains('is-revealed')` was `false`, meaning the scroll-reveal system never fired for this block, ever — every `.reveal-up`/`.reveal-item` child was stuck at its initial `opacity:0` state permanently, on every page, since the day scroll-reveal was first wired into this block (round 3, Sep 14 — not something introduced by today's round 4 work).

**Root cause.** `resource-downloads/block.json` sets `"supports": { "className": false, "customClassName": false }` — a pre-existing, deliberate setting (stops editors from hand-adding a custom class to this data-driven block) that has a side effect nobody accounted for when scroll-reveal was wired in: it also stops WordPress's block-supports system from ever attaching the *default* `wp-block-cropx-resource-downloads` wrapper class that `get_block_wrapper_attributes()` adds to every other block automatically. Every version of this block's `view.js` — round 3's single-selector version and today's per-subsection version alike — called `initScrollReveal('.wp-block-cropx-resource-downloads .rsd-header, .wp-block-cropx-resource-downloads .rsd-subsection')`. Since that ancestor class was never actually on the page, `querySelectorAll()` always returned an empty list, `initScrollReveal()` never called `.observe()` on anything, and nothing was ever marked `is-revealed`. Confirmed directly in the browser: `document.querySelector('.wp-block-cropx-resource-downloads')` returns `null` on the live page, while `document.querySelector('.rsd-header')` and `.rsd-subsection` (6 of them) exist fine.

**Second block found broken by the same bug, before Lauren even reported it.** Grepped every block.json in `src/blocks/*/` for the same `"className": false` setting and found it on exactly one other block that currently has scroll-reveal wired: **Photo Grid** — shipped in today's round 4, so this bug had been live for under an hour. Its `view.js` called `initScrollReveal('.wp-block-cropx-photo-grid')`, same failure mode. (A third block, Field Photos, also has `className: false` but doesn't have scroll-reveal wired at all yet, so it's unaffected today — worth remembering before ever adding scroll-reveal to it.)

**Fix.** Rewrote both `view.js` selectors to target the block's own semantic class directly instead of the (never-present) `wp-block-cropx-*` ancestor — no functional loss, since `.rsd-header`/`.rsd-subsection` and `.pgd-section` are already unique to their respective blocks and don't need a namespace prefix to avoid collisions:
- **Resource Downloads:** `'.wp-block-cropx-resource-downloads .rsd-header, .wp-block-cropx-resource-downloads .rsd-subsection'` → `'.rsd-header, .rsd-subsection'`
- **Photo Grid:** `'.wp-block-cropx-photo-grid'` → `'.pgd-section'`

Hand-patched both compiled `build/blocks/*/view.js` files identically (this sandbox still can't run `npm run build`), verified with `node --check`, and grepped to confirm zero remaining references to the broken selectors anywhere in `build/`.

**Deploy.** `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.61` → `0.4.62`. This is a pure JS-selector fix — no new files, no markup changes, no CSS changes — so the patch is minimal: `functions.php` + the two blocks' `view.js` files only. Packaged `resource-grid-photo-grid-reveal-fix-deploy.zip` / `resource-grid-photo-grid-reveal-fix-revert.zip` (revert reconstructed by re-inserting the broken selectors — verified via `node --check` and an exact string-swap script). No `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt` needed this time: unlike prior rounds, no new files were added, so the revert zip's plain overwrite fully restores the pre-fix state on its own.

**Action for Lauren:** extract `resource-grid-photo-grid-reveal-fix-deploy.zip` over `wp-content/themes/cropx/` via WP File Manager — this should immediately restore the Resource Grid content on every page that uses it (Brochures & Info Sheets, and any Resource Grid instance elsewhere on the site), plus fix Photo Grid's reveal before anyone notices it independently.

## September 14, 2026 — Scroll-reveal animation: per-subsection independent reveal (People Showcase + Resource Downloads)

**Why.** After approving round 3 (below), Lauren asked: "For the People Showcase block and other blocks that can have multiple sub-sections (e.g. 'Global Management', 'Business Leads', 'Board of Directors'... on the About page) is it possible to have each section appear individually as the user scrolls down?" — then clarified she meant `https://new.cropx.com/about/company/`, which uses People Showcase with three team groups (Global Management Team, Business Leads, Board of Directors). Previously the entire block (every group at once) faded in together the moment the block's top edge scrolled into view — a visitor scrolling past "Global Management" would already see "Board of Directors" cards fully revealed off-screen below. Lauren's ask was for each group to fade in independently, on its own, as the visitor scrolls to IT specifically. She also asked generally about "other blocks that can have multiple sub-sections" — Resource Downloads was the only other currently-wired block with this same repeatable-subsection shape (its `groups` attribute, some collapsible), so it got the identical fix without being asked by name, since the extra work was minimal.

**The CSS trap that made this non-trivial.** The shared `initScrollReveal(selector)` utility (`src/shared/scrollReveal.js`) already supports independent per-element reveal — it calls `querySelectorAll(selector)` and observes every match separately, so simply widening the selector to match each subsection individually looked like it should be enough. It wasn't: `scroll-reveal.css`'s rule is `html.cropx-reveal-js .reveal-group.is-revealed .reveal-item` — a descendant combinator, which matches ANY matching ancestor, not just the nearest one. Both blocks had `reveal-group` on the *outer* section wrapper, so nesting a second `reveal-group` on each subsection inside it would have been pointless: the instant the outer wrapper's `is-revealed` landed, every card in every subsection would satisfy the CSS rule regardless of whether its own subsection had been scrolled to yet. The fix was to remove `reveal-group` from the outer section entirely and move it onto each individual subsection div instead, so each one is its own independent observed root with no ancestor leaking a stale `is-revealed` down to it. The header (eyebrow/heading/body) doesn't need `reveal-group` at all since `.reveal-up` only needs *any* revealed ancestor — it's now just its own separately-observed element (`.section-header` / `.rsd-header`), decoupled entirely from the outer section wrapper's classing.

**Wiring, per block:**
- **People Showcase** (`src/blocks/people-showcase/`): `render.php` — outer `<section>` no longer gets `reveal-group`; each `.people-group` div now gets `reveal-group` directly. The per-card delay counter (`$ps_card_counter`) now resets at the top of every group's loop iteration instead of running across all groups, and the base delay shifts to `0.15s` when that specific group shows a heading or `0.05s` when it doesn't (so cards don't wait behind a heading that isn't rendered) — group headings themselves get `reveal-up` at `0.05s`. `view.js` — `initScrollReveal()` now passes two selectors: `.section-header, .people-group`, so the header and every group are independently observed roots instead of one call against the whole block wrapper.
- **Resource Downloads** (`src/blocks/resource-downloads/`): identical pattern. `render.php` — `reveal-group` moved off the outer `.rsd-section` onto each `.rsd-subsection` div; per-card counter resets per subsection with the same conditional 0.15s/0.05s base; both subheading variants (collapsible-button and plain) get `reveal-up` at `0.05s`. `view.js` — selector widened to `.rsd-header, .rsd-subsection`. Double-checked collapsed-by-default subsections stay safe under this change: the newly-independent observed root is the subsection's outer wrapper, which always has real height and sits in normal document flow regardless of whether its *inner* content track is collapsed (`grid-template-rows: 0fr`) — so `is-revealed` still lands the moment a visitor scrolls that subsection into view, same as before this change.

**Build workaround (npm run build still broken in this sandbox):** copied both updated `render.php` files verbatim into `build/` (verified byte-identical via `diff`). Hand-patched both compiled `view.js` files' minified `querySelectorAll(...)` selector strings to match the new two-part selectors, verified with `node --check`. `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.59` → `0.4.60`. Packaged both a forward deploy zip and a revert-backup zip (reconstructed back to the exact pre-round state — outer-wrapper `reveal-group`, no per-subsection independent triggering, counters running across all groups/subsections rather than resetting per one) — `scroll-reveal-round3-deploy.zip` / `scroll-reveal-round3-revert.zip` (named "round3" as a zip filename — this is chronologically the 4th scroll-reveal round overall, following `scroll-reveal-round2-*`).

**Scroll-reveal is now live on 9 blocks** (unchanged from round 3's count) — this round only changed the *triggering granularity* for 2 of them, not which blocks have it.

## September 14, 2026 — Scroll-reveal animation: round 3 — Three-Column Icons, Icon Columns, Resource Downloads, Pricing Tiers, People Showcase

**Why.** Lauren asked for another 3-4 blocks after confirming round 2 + the timing fix looked good. Recommended Three-Column Icons / Icon Columns, Resource Downloads, Pricing Tiers, and People Showcase — all share the same proven eyebrow+heading+repeated-item shape, and none of them have a conflicting animation system already (unlike Two-Column Animated, which was excluded for that reason; hero blocks were also excluded since they already have their own load-time fade-up, and nav/footer/menu-links/footer-brand are chrome, not content sections). Lauren's approval named "Three-Column Icons / Icon Columns" ambiguously as one bullet covering two distinct, both-currently-live block types — rather than guess which one she meant, wired scroll-reveal into **both**, since the extra work was minimal given how nearly identical their structure is. So this round covers 5 blocks total.

**Wiring — same pattern as every prior round, per block:**
- **Three-Column Icons / Icon Columns** (`src/blocks/three-column-icons/`, `src/blocks/icon-columns/`): `reveal-group` on the section wrapper; eyebrow/heading get `reveal-up` (0.05s/0.15s); the item loop in both gained an `$idx` index so each `.tci-item`/`.ici-item` gets `reveal-item` + a per-index `--reveal-delay` (`0.3 + min(idx,8)×0.06s`). Icon Columns' `view.js` already does something clever post-render — it regroups the flat `.ici-item` DOM nodes into `.ici-col` wrapper divs for a count-balanced multi-column layout. Confirmed this is safe with reveal: the regroup only ever *moves* existing nodes (`appendChild()` on nodes already in the DOM), never recreates them, so each item's `reveal-item` class/inline delay/animation state rides along untouched through the move. Both blocks needed a first-ever `view.js` created (Three-Column Icons) or an existing one extended (Icon Columns) to call `initScrollReveal()`.
- **Resource Downloads** (`src/blocks/resource-downloads/`): same `reveal-group`/`reveal-up` header treatment. This block's cards render inside repeatable subsection groups, some of which can be collapsed by default (`grid-template-rows: 0fr` + `inert`) — worth double-checking the reveal animation wouldn't get stuck on cards inside a collapsed panel. It doesn't: the CSS cascade (`.reveal-group.is-revealed .reveal-item`) keys off DOM ancestry to the *outer* section wrapper, not the card's own current visibility, and the outer wrapper always has real height and enters the viewport normally regardless of which subsections are collapsed — so `is-revealed` lands immediately and every card's fade-up plays out to its settled `opacity:1` state well within the first second, long before anyone could expand a collapsed panel to see it happen mid-animation. Per-card delay uses a running counter that is **not** reset per subsection group, so the stagger reads naturally top-to-bottom even across a page with multiple subsections.
- **Pricing Tiers** (`src/blocks/pricing-tiers/`): same header treatment; tier-card loop gained `$idx` for `reveal-item` + delay on each `.pt-card`.
- **People Showcase** (`src/blocks/people-showcase/`): this block has a 3-element header (eyebrow/heading/**body**, not just eyebrow/heading), so all three get `reveal-up` at 0.05s/0.15s/0.25s and the per-card item base shifts to 0.35s to sit after them. Team-member cards render inside repeatable "people-group" sections (same shape as Resource Downloads' subsections) — same running-counter-not-reset-per-group treatment for the stagger delay.
- All 5 blocks got the shared scroll-reveal CSS inlined into their `style.css` (copy-paste, not import — see Sep 13 entry for why) and the settled-state-in-editor-canvas override added to their `editor.css`, matching every prior round's convention exactly.

**Build workaround (npm run build still broken in this sandbox):** copied all 5 updated `render.php` files verbatim into `build/` (verified byte-identical via `diff`). Created `build/blocks/{three-column-icons,pricing-tiers,people-showcase}/view.js` + `view.asset.php` from scratch (the minified single-purpose scroll-reveal IIFE, same pattern as prior rounds' from-scratch view.js files) and added `"viewScript"` to their compiled `block.json`. Prepended that same minified IIFE onto `icon-columns/view.js` and `resource-downloads/view.js`'s existing compiled content (both already had a view.js for other reasons). Appended the minified scroll-reveal CSS to all 5 blocks' compiled `style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css`, and the settled-state override to their compiled `index.css`. Verified every JS file with `node --check`, every JSON file with `json.load()`, and brace/tag balance on every touched CSS/PHP file.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.58` → `0.4.59`. Packaged both a forward deploy zip and a revert-backup zip (reconstructed by reversing every known edit back to the exact pre-round, 0.4.58 state) — `scroll-reveal-round2-deploy.zip` / `scroll-reveal-round2-revert.zip` (named "round2" as a zip filename to avoid colliding with the existing `stats-grid-faq-accordion-reveal-*` round-2 zips already in the folder; this is chronologically the 3rd scroll-reveal round overall). **Revert note:** Three-Column Icons, Pricing Tiers, and People Showcase had no `view.js` before this round — since a zip extraction can only add/overwrite files, not delete them, the revert zip includes a `REVERT-INSTRUCTIONS.txt` calling out that those 3 blocks' `view.js` + `view.asset.php` need to be deleted by hand in File Manager after extracting, mirroring the exact same caveat from the Sep 13 Cards-block revert.

**Scroll-reveal is now live on 9 of the site's blocks:** Cards, Testimonials Carousel, Stats Grid, FAQ Accordion, Three-Column Icons, Icon Columns, Resource Downloads, Pricing Tiers, People Showcase. **Still pending Lauren's go-ahead:** which blocks (if any) to do next.

## September 14, 2026 — Scroll-reveal animation: timing fix + Stats Grid body/CTA gap closed (Lauren feedback on round 2)

**Why.** Lauren reviewed the round-2 deploy (Stats Grid + FAQ Accordion, immediately below) live and reported two problems: "the paragraph text and CTA are just THERE in the stats grid when I scroll down to that block – they don't animate in like the rest of the content," and separately, "for all blocks it seems like there's a little bit too much of a pause before things animate in. Is there some kind of delay on the animation starting?" Both are fixed in this round, across all four blocks that currently use scroll-reveal (Cards, Testimonials Carousel, Stats Grid, FAQ Accordion) — not just the two from round 2.

**Bug 1 — Stats Grid's body copy and CTA were never wired into the reveal system.** Confirmed in `src/blocks/stats-grid/render.php`: eyebrow and heading got `reveal-up` in round 2, but `.section-body` and both CTA variants (`.sg-btn` button style, `.sg-cta` link style) had no `reveal-up` class or `--reveal-delay` style at all — an oversight in that round's wiring, not something Lauren asked to change. Fixed by adding `reveal-up` + delays to both: body at `0.25s`, CTA at `0.35s`, slotting in after the existing eyebrow (`0.05s`)/heading (`0.15s`) delays. The per-card stat delay formula shifted its base from `0.4s` to account for the now-4-deep header stagger.

**Bug 2 — the "too much of a pause" was the shared IntersectionObserver, not the CSS.** `src/shared/scrollReveal.js`'s observer was configured `{ threshold: 0.15, rootMargin: '0px 0px -10% 0px' }` — a section had to be scrolled 15% into view *and* past a boundary shrunk 10% off the real viewport bottom before the observer fired at all. Combined with the CSS stagger, this meant a block could sit fully visible on screen, inert, for a beat before anything moved — reads exactly like the "delay" Lauren described. Loosened to `{ threshold: 0.01, rootMargin: '0px' }` so reveal starts the moment any part of the section touches the viewport. Also compressed the CSS stagger schedule across all 4 blocks — header eyebrow/heading moved from `0.2s`/`0.35s` to `0.05s`/`0.15s`, and every per-item stagger formula (cards, testimonial cards, stat cards, FAQ items) tightened from `0.45s + index×0.08s` to a `0.3s`-ish base + `index×0.06s` (Stats Grid's base is `0.4s` since it now has 4 header elements ahead of the cards instead of 2).

**Build workaround (still no working `npm run build` in this sandbox):** hand-patched the same 4 files' worth of changes into `build/` — copied all 4 blocks' updated `render.php` verbatim from `src/` (verified byte-identical via `diff`), and hand-edited the minified IntersectionObserver options string (`threshold:.15,rootMargin:"0px 0px -10% 0px"` → `threshold:.01,rootMargin:"0px"`) inside each of the 4 compiled `view.js` files — Testimonials Carousel and FAQ Accordion each have a second, unrelated IIFE in the same file (carousel-nav, accordion click-handler) that was left untouched. Verified every touched JS file with `node --check` and confirmed no stray copies of the old observer string remained anywhere in `build/`.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.57` → `0.4.58`. Packaged both a forward deploy zip and a revert-backup zip (revert reconstructed by reversing every known edit back to the exact round-2 state, including Stats Grid's body/CTA having no `reveal-up` at all) — `stats-grid-faq-accordion-reveal-timing-fix-deploy.zip` / `stats-grid-faq-accordion-reveal-timing-fix-revert.zip`. Same small-targeted-patch deploy method as prior rounds: extract directly over `wp-content/themes/cropx/`, `functions.php` at the theme root and all 4 block folders under `build/blocks/`.

Asked what to test next; recommended Stats Grid + FAQ Accordion as the next pair since both are common, simple sections (one static stat grid, one interactive accordion) that exercise the pattern in slightly different DOM shapes without much risk.

**Wiring — identical pattern to Cards/Testimonials Carousel, per block:**
- **Stats Grid** (`src/blocks/stats-grid/`): `reveal-group` added to the section wrapper class; eyebrow/heading get `reveal-up` with 0.2s/0.35s delays; the stat-card loop was changed from `foreach ( $stats as $stat )` to `foreach ( $stats as $idx => $stat )` so each `.sg-stat-card` can get `reveal-item` + a per-index `--reveal-delay` (`0.45 + min(idx,8)×0.08`, same formula as Cards/Testimonials Carousel). This block had no `view.js` at all before — created one whose only job is calling `initScrollReveal('.wp-block-cropx-stats-grid')`, and added `"viewScript": "file:./view.js"` to `block.json` (wasn't registered before).
- **FAQ Accordion** (`src/blocks/faq-accordion/`): same `reveal-group`/`reveal-up` wiring on the wrapper and header. The accordion-item loop already used `foreach ( $items as $idx => $item )` for its ARIA id suffix, so no loop-signature change was needed there — just added `reveal-item` + `--reveal-delay` to each `.faq-item`. This block already had a `view.js` (single-open accordion click handler) — added the `initScrollReveal('.wp-block-cropx-faq-accordion')` import/call alongside the existing logic rather than replacing it. One care point: FAQ Accordion's eyebrow already had a conditional inline `style="color:..."` attribute for the deep-blue-bg text-color override — folded the new `--reveal-delay` custom property into that *same* style attribute rather than adding a second `style=` attribute on the element (which would be invalid HTML and unpredictable across browsers).
- Both blocks got the shared scroll-reveal CSS block inlined into their `style.css` (copy-paste, not import — see Sep 13 entry for why this project's webpack build requires that) and the "settled state in the editor canvas" override added to their `editor.css`, matching the Cards/Testimonials Carousel convention exactly.

**Build workaround (npm run build still broken in this sandbox):** hand-patched `build/blocks/stats-grid/` and `build/blocks/faq-accordion/` — copied the updated `render.php` files verbatim, created `build/blocks/stats-grid/view.js` + `view.asset.php` from scratch (mirroring the minified single-purpose scroll-reveal IIFE already used in Cards' compiled `view.js`), prepended that same minified IIFE onto FAQ Accordion's existing compiled `view.js` (ahead of its accordion click-handler IIFE — same two-IIFEs-back-to-back pattern Testimonials Carousel's compiled `view.js` already has), added `"viewScript"` to the compiled `stats-grid/block.json`, and prepended/appended the minified scroll-reveal CSS block (front-end) and settled-state override (editor) to both blocks' compiled `style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css`/`index.css`. Verified via `node --check` on all JS, `json.load()` on both `block.json` files, and open-brace/close-brace balance checks on every touched CSS file — all passed, plus confirmed the two hand-copied `render.php` files are byte-identical to their `src/` originals.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.56` → `0.4.57`. Packaged both a forward deploy zip and a revert-backup zip (reconstructed from the pre-edit file contents, since this project has no git history to diff against) — `stats-grid-faq-accordion-reveal-deploy.zip` / `stats-grid-faq-accordion-reveal-revert.zip`. Deploy via the small-targeted-patch method (CLAUDE.md): extract directly over `wp-content/themes/cropx/`, `functions.php` at the theme root and the two block folders under `build/blocks/`.

## September 14, 2026 — Unicode-escape corruption RECURRED despite the "Manual Translation Only" fix — fixed live, root trigger still unconfirmed

**What happened.** Lauren reported the homepage hero was back to showing raw `u003cemu003ecompleteu003c/emu003e` — the same corruption signature as the Sep 9–11 incident (see below), months after "Manual Translation Only" was checked in TranslatePress specifically to stop this. **Confirmed via wp-admin (logged in as Lauren, not through the rebuilt admin tool — that tool was deleted Sep 11 after being marked resolved) that the setting is still checked.** So either TranslatePress has a bug where this setting doesn't fully cover the trigger, or something else entirely caused this recurrence. Root cause is **not confirmed** — this is flagged as open follow-up work, not resolved.

**Scope.** A REST API sweep across all 8 relevant post types (posts, pages, wp_block/Synced Patterns, and all 5 custom post types) found 52 posts/pages with suspicious tokens, but about 21 of those turned out to be false positives from a blunter first-pass check (content that legitimately contains an escaped `’` etc. with the backslash intact — never corrupted). The real damage: **31 posts/pages/patterns**, spanning both a fresh wave from today (2026-09-14, tightly clustered around 17:23 UTC — same "one automated sweep" signature as the original incident) and a smaller batch of **stale, never-actually-fixed corruption dating back to Sep 10–11** that the "zero remaining issues" scan at the end of that incident apparently missed (likely because it didn't cover all 5 custom post types, or missed Synced Patterns specifically).

**Fixed with a safer method than the original admin tool.** Investigated *why* the original Unicode Escape Repair tool caused its own "disappeared elements" regression (Sep 2026, task history) and traced it to a real bug in that tool's approach: it replaced corrupted tokens (`u003c`, `u0022`, etc.) with the **literal character** (`<`, `"`, etc.) directly inside the block comment's JSON attribute string. For most tokens that's harmless, but for `u0022` specifically, inserting a raw unescaped `"` into a JSON string **breaks the JSON** (premature string termination) — silently corrupting or dropping the whole block. Confirmed by inspecting raw content: these tokens always sit inside `<!-- wp:block {"attr":"...u003c..."} -->` JSON spans, e.g. `{"heading":"The u003cemu003ecompleteu003c/emu003e..."}`. The **correct, safe fix is to restore the missing backslash** (`u003c` → `<`) rather than substitute the raw character — this exactly reverses whatever stripped it, is always valid JSON regardless of context, and lets WordPress's own block-comment JSON decoder do the character conversion naturally at render time. Same logic applied to the "nn" paragraph-break pattern: restored as `\n\n` (escaped, matching valid JSON), not a literal newline byte (which is invalid inside a JSON string and was likely the actual cause of the original regression).

**Execution:** ran directly via the WP REST API from an authenticated admin session (Lauren logged into wp-admin in her own Chrome, work done through Claude in Chrome) rather than rebuilding the deleted admin tool — for a one-time emergency fix, faster and lets every candidate be individually JSON-validated (extracted every `<!-- wp:... {...} -->` span from the fixed content and ran `JSON.parse()` on each) before saving, rather than trusting a blind regex. 31 posts saved via `POST /wp/v2/<type>/<id>`, 21 correctly left untouched, 0 validation failures, 0 save errors. Re-scanned sitewide afterward: zero remaining corrupted tokens across all 8 post types. Spot-checked the homepage live — renders cleanly (hero headline, ampersands in the stats band, the "nn"-corrupted contact-form intro copy, all correct).

**Not resolved — flagged for Lauren:** the trigger is still unknown. "Manual Translation Only" being checked and this still happening means either TranslatePress has a bug, or the real cause is something else that happens to produce the identical tight-timestamp-batch signature (a plugin doing a bulk resave — Yoast Duplicate Post's Rewrite & Republish was newly installed since Sep 11 and is worth suspecting, as is All-in-One WP Migration or UpdraftPlus if either ran an import/restore). No admin repair tool exists anymore for a fast recovery next time — worth deciding whether to rebuild it (with this safer backslash-restore logic) given this is now a 3rd occurrence, or keep handling it as a one-off emergency fix each time.

**Diagnostic test started same day:** since the site isn't fully public yet, Lauren approved deactivating both TranslatePress plugins entirely (TranslatePress - Multilingual v3.3.5 and TranslatePress - Business v1.8.6) as of **2026-09-14, shortly after the fix above** — the most decisive test available. If corruption does NOT recur while both are off, that's conclusive proof TranslatePress (despite "Manual Translation Only") is the cause. If it DOES recur with both plugins inactive, the real cause is elsewhere (Yoast Duplicate Post, a migration/backup plugin, or something else) and TranslatePress should be reactivated and cleared as a suspect. **Known side effect for the test window:** the live English/Español language switcher in the nav is gone while these are off — expected and accepted by Lauren. Next session should check whether corruption has recurred since this timestamp before doing anything else with TranslatePress.

Left Team Member's LinkedIn URL and Dealer's Website fields (2 places) as `type="url"` since those should always be absolute external links.
