## September 13, 2026 — Scroll-triggered reveal animation: shared utility built, wired into Cards + Testimonials Carousel (step 1 of incremental sitewide rollout)

**Why.** Lauren asked for all blocks to get the same "animate in as it scrolls into view" treatment the curved hero blocks already have (eyebrow/heading/subheading/CTA fading up on a stagger), but explicitly asked to roll it out **a couple of blocks at a time** so any block that "doesn't play nice" with the animation can be reversed without touching the others.

**Key architecture decision — this can't just copy the hero pattern.** The hero blocks' animation is a pure CSS `animation: ... both` that fires on page load with staggered delays — it only looks scroll-triggered because a hero is always the first thing in the viewport. A real scroll-into-view effect for below-the-fold blocks needs actual scroll detection, so this is a new IntersectionObserver-based system, not a copy of hero's CSS-only approach. Flagged this distinction to Lauren before starting so the two systems aren't confused later.

**Shared utility (inert until a block opts in — this is what makes per-block revert safe):**
- `src/shared/scrollReveal.js` — one shared `IntersectionObserver` instance (`threshold: 0.15`, `rootMargin: '0px 0px -10% 0px'`) watches whatever selector a block's `view.js` passes to `initScrollReveal()`. Once ~15% into view, adds `is-revealed` to that element and stops observing it — one-shot, doesn't replay on scroll back up.
- `src/shared/scroll-reveal.css` — **reference copy only, not imported directly** (same documented reason as `un-goals-two-column/style.css`: this project's webpack build never gets shared CSS into a block's compiled stylesheet, whether via native `@import` or via importing CSS from JS — importing from view.js routes it into the editor-only bundle regardless of which file does the importing). Each consuming block pastes an identical copy of this CSS into its own `style.css`, using the same class/keyframe names every time (`reveal-up`, `reveal-group`/`reveal-item`, `cropx-reveal-fade-up`) so nothing collides.
- Progressive enhancement, no `<noscript>` needed: elements are only hidden once `html.cropx-reveal-js` is present, and only the JS itself adds that class right before it starts observing. No-JS visitors never get anything hidden.
- `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` respected, matching the hero-family convention.
- **Bug caught before shipping:** first draft's CSS selector was `.reveal-up.is-revealed` (both classes on the same element) — but `is-revealed` only ever lands on the observed *wrapper*, while `.reveal-up`/`.reveal-item` are descendants, so the animation would never have fired on any real block. Changed to the descendant combinator (`.is-revealed .reveal-up`) before wiring into Cards or Testimonials Carousel — no user-facing impact.
- Editor-canvas override convention (matches hero-family's own `editor.css` pattern, but adapted): each consuming block's `editor.css` forces `opacity: 1 !important; transform: none !important; animation: none !important;` on `.reveal-up`/`.reveal-item` — the extra `opacity`/`transform` reset (hero-family only needed `animation: none`) is because this system's resting state is a *static* `opacity:0`, not purely animation-driven, so `animation: none` alone would leave elements invisible in the editor.

**Wired into 2 blocks this round (Lauren's own selection — cards + testimonials-carousel), staggering each repeated item individually (her preference over fading the whole group as one unit):**
- **Cards** — wrapper gets `reveal-group`; eyebrow (0.2s) and heading (0.35s) get `reveal-up`; each card gets `reveal-item` with a per-index delay (`0.45s + card_index × 0.08s`, capped at index 8 so a long list doesn't push the tail out to a multi-second delay).
- **Testimonials Carousel** — same structure: wrapper `reveal-group`, eyebrow (0.2s) + heading (0.35s) `reveal-up`, each testimonial card `reveal-item` with the same `0.45 + index×0.08` stagger (capped at index 8).
- Both blocks' `view.js` now call `initScrollReveal()` on their own root selector; Cards needed a brand-new `view.js` + `viewScript` registration in `block.json` (it had none before), Testimonials Carousel already had a `view.js` so the call was added inside its existing IIFE.

**Build-tool workaround (recurring sandbox issue).** `npm run build` / `npx wp-scripts build` still fails in this sandbox with `sh: 1: wp-scripts: Resource deadlock avoided` — reproduced again on retry, same filesystem-level issue reading through the `node_modules/.bin` symlink seen earlier in the project. Worked around by hand-patching the compiled `build/blocks/cards/` and `build/blocks/testimonials-carousel/` output files directly to match what the build would have produced (render.php copied byte-identical from src since it has no compile step; JS minified by hand to match webpack's output shape; CSS appended in minified single-line form to both `style-index.css` and `style-index-rtl.css`). Verified via `node --check` on all JS, `json.load()` on both `block.json` files, and brace-count balance on all CSS — all clean.

`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped `0.4.54` → `0.4.55` (functions.php) per the cache-busting gotcha (CLAUDE.md #10) — required since both blocks' CSS/JS changed.

**Shipped as two zips** (small targeted patch, per CLAUDE.md's WP File Manager convention):
- **Forward patch** — `functions.php` + full `build/blocks/cards/` + full `build/blocks/testimonials-carousel/` folders (25 files). Extract directly over `wp-content/themes/cropx/` in File Manager.
- **Revert backup** — pre-change `functions.php` + the pre-change render.php/CSS/block.json/view.js for both blocks (13 files), reconstructed by hand since this project has no git and no snapshot was taken before editing (reversed every known edit via scripted string replacement, then verified with a full `grep -rl "reveal"` sweep across the reconstructed tree returning zero hits). **Reverting needs one manual step beyond just extracting this zip**: `build/blocks/cards/view.js` and `build/blocks/cards/view.asset.php` are brand-new files this round (Cards had no `view.js` before), so a zip extraction can't remove them — delete those two files by hand in File Manager if reverting Cards specifically.

**Next.** This is step 1 of the incremental rollout Lauren asked for. Once she confirms Cards and Testimonials Carousel look right live, continue wiring `scrollReveal.js`/`scroll-reveal.css` into more blocks two at a time, following the same pattern (wrapper → `reveal-group`, eyebrow/heading → `reveal-up`, repeated items → `reveal-item` with per-index stagger). Don't start the next pair without Lauren's go-ahead on this one first — that pacing is the whole point of doing this incrementally.

**Hero visual.** After an isometric-illustration pass was rejected ("Photorealism please"), rebuilt `assets/images/photos/cropx-sensor-ecosystem-hero.webp` on the original real-photo-composite pipeline (real device cutouts + real farm background), not the vector/SVG approach — SVG illustration can't produce photorealism no matter how refined. Added layered Gaussian-blur cast/contact shadows under every device and info card, and replaced the flat vector satellite icon with a gradient-shaded, "rendered" satellite (metallic body, angled solar panels, sun-glint, motion trail). Rendered via cairosvg, converted to RGB before optimizing (avoids a false `ALPHA_PRESERVED` flag from anti-aliasing edge pixels), delivered at 2000×778 WebP.

**"CropX System" → "CropX Platform" rename.** Early copy called the product "the CropX System"; current brand language is "the CropX Platform." Two-part fix, same shape as the Unicode Escape Repair precedent below (source files vs. live database are separate problems):
- **Theme source files fixed directly** (7 `patterns/*.php` files, 3 `src/blocks/*/block.json` defaults — plus their built `build/blocks/*/block.json` copies, since `npm run build` wasn't available in-session so those 3 build folders were hand-patched with the identical one-line change — a `style.css` comment, and `archive-cropx_resource.php`'s doc comment). Case-preserving regex (`\bcropx(\s+)system(s?)\b`, replace only "system" → "platform" matching the original's case) applied via a small Python script (`/tmp/rename_system_to_platform.py`) and verified with a full re-grep afterward: zero "CropX System" left, all new "CropX Platform" occurrences correctly cased. The static `blocks/*.html` mockups (`landing-page-f4.html`, `three-column-icons.html`, `resource-card-language-picker-options.html`) got the same fix.
- **New admin tool for live content:** `inc/cropx-system-to-platform-rename.php` (Tools → CropX System → Platform Rename), modeled on `unicode-escape-repair.php`'s scan/apply architecture — scans `post_content` across all post types/statuses including `wp_block` (Synced Patterns), shows before/after context, applies the same case-preserving rename on confirmation. Unlike the escape-repair tool, this one does NOT need JSON-attribute-span awareness — swapping "system" for "platform" introduces no quotes/brackets/backslashes, so a plain find-and-replace is safe everywhere in post_content. Post *titles* are only ever flagged for manual review, never auto-renamed — at least one resource is titled "CropX System – Detailed Overview" and a title change could desync it from the underlying PDF filename/slug. Relies on WordPress's own post revisions for rollback, same as unicode-escape-repair.php.
- Shipped as `system-to-platform-rename-deploy.zip` (functions.php + new inc file + 3 build/blocks folders + archive-cropx_resource.php + 7 pattern files) — extract into `wp-content/themes/cropx/` on staging, no npm build needed since the build folders are already compiled.
- **Not fixed / flagged, not in scope:** `wp-theme/cropx/src/blocks/{zoho-contact-form,zoho-form}/block.json` still default to the known-broken "TestForm" formperma placeholder URL — latent risk only if a brand-new Zoho form block is inserted without explicitly setting its URL. Worth a follow-up.

**Removed 3 resolved one-time admin tools** (Lauren confirmed all affected content already fixed): `inc/fix-zoho-formperma.php`, `inc/relative-media-urls.php`, `inc/unicode-escape-repair.php` deleted; their three guarded `require_once` blocks in `functions.php` replaced with one explanatory comment. Shipped as `updated-functions-php-patch.zip` (forward patch — just overwrite `functions.php`) plus `revert-3-admin-tools-backup.zip` (the 3 original files + the pre-removal `functions.php`, in case any of them are needed again). **Staging deploy note:** a patch zip only adds/overwrites files on extraction, it never deletes — so after uploading, the three old `inc/*.php` files need to be deleted by hand via WP File Manager, or they'll sit there orphaned (harmless, just dead weight, since nothing references them anymore).

## September 11, 2026 — ROOT CAUSE FOUND: TranslatePress was the sitewide corruption source all along. Recovered again, and turned off the trigger.

**What happened.** Hours after the Sep 11 escaped-newline-run fix (entry below) was verified live and clean, Lauren reported the homepage hero was back to showing raw `u003cemu003ecompleteu003c/emu003e` — the exact original Sep 9/10 corruption, not a new variant. Investigating the homepage's revision history confirmed it: revision 4293, saved at **2026-09-11T17:42:08 UTC under Lauren's own WordPress account**, contained the fully raw, unescaped corruption — as if the entire Sep 10 recovery had never happened.

**First theory (wrong, corrected quickly):** initially suspected a stale, long-open block-editor tab silently overwriting the fix on save (WordPress's editor doesn't refresh from server-side changes, so an old tab's "Update" click can clobber newer content). Ruled this out by checking modification timestamps across every affected page: **24 pages, including the homepage, were all modified within the same 4-second window (17:45:13–17:45:17 UTC)** — including pages that had never been part of any prior incident (Careers, Contact Us, CropX Vision, Disease Control, Telemetry, several hardware product pages, both SCIO pages, two acquisition announcement pages, and a patterns utility page). No one clicks "Update" on 24 unrelated pages in 4 seconds — this was one automated process sweeping the database.

**Actual root cause: TranslatePress.** Settings → TranslatePress → Advanced → Miscellaneous options has a setting called **"Manual Translation Only"**, off by default, whose own description reads: *"TranslatePress pro-actively scans and saves strings in the database when users access translated pages."* This is a visit-triggered background write, not a schedule — any traffic hitting translated versions of pages (a crawler, a bot, an SEO tool, Google itself re-indexing `/es/` URLs) causes TranslatePress to rescan and resave that post's content into its own tables. Something in that save path strips backslashes from the JSON-escaped block attributes, exactly reproducing the "u003c" and "nn" corruption — this is almost certainly what caused the *original* Sep 9–10 incident too, not TranslatePress/DeepL machine translation or All-in-One WP Migration as originally suspected (both were "leading suspects" then; this pinpoints the actual mechanism). **Fix: checked "Manual Translation Only" and saved.** This stops the automatic background scan-and-save entirely — translation now only happens deliberately inside the Translation Editor. Should prevent recurrence going forward; worth monitoring for a few days to confirm.

**Recovery, in two passes:**
1. **24 pages** with either fully-raw tokens (`u003c`/`u003e`/`u0026` etc. with no backslash) or the tag-boundary escaped-newline-run pattern (`>` + literal "n"/"nn" + `<`) — fixed via a 3-pass comment-aware script run directly through the REST API (same technique as Sep 10): (1) fix bare-token n-runs first, (2) comment-aware safe-token fix, (3) fix any remaining already-escaped-tag n-runs. Every post's JSON blocks validated with `JSON.parse()` before saving.
2. **36 pages** (a much wider set — this Synced text is duplicated, not referenced, across most of the site) found with the punctuation-adjacent-capital "nn" pattern (e.g. "...within a few days.nnOr if you prefer...") — this is the same pattern the tool has always held for manual review rather than auto-fixing, but since it's the exact same already-vetted, unambiguous signature from Sep 10, Lauren gave the go-ahead to batch-fix all 36 directly via REST using the tool's own comment-aware nn-fix logic.

**Verification:** a full sitewide re-scan across all 8 relevant post types for all three corruption signatures (bare tokens, escaped-tag n-runs, punctuation-nn) came back with **zero remaining issues**. Spot-checked the homepage and the Australia contact page live — both render cleanly.

**Lesson:** when a sitewide content-corruption bug reappears identically after being fixed, check for a **trigger**, not just a recurrence of the original cause — the fact that 24 unrelated pages changed within a 4-second window was the tell that this was an automated, visit-triggered process rather than a repeat of the original unknown event. TranslatePress's automatic background re-scan-and-save is now confirmed as at minimum a contributing mechanism (possibly the sole original cause) and is disabled via "Manual Translation Only."

---

## September 11, 2026 — Unicode Escape Repair tool: second corruption signature found and fixed (escaped-newline runs between tag boundaries)

**What Lauren found.** A screenshot of the "Green Brain Irrigation Management" content card on a live regional page showed literal "nn" and "n" text sitting on their own stray lines between an intro paragraph and a bulleted list, and between individual bullets.

**Root cause — the same stripped-backslash bug, a different escape token.** This is a second manifestation of the exact same Sep 10 root cause (see the entry below), just at a different granularity. The corrupted string was `...u003e/pu003enu003cpu003e...` — read literally, `</p>` + a lone "n" + `<p>`. The "n" isn't stray text; it's the paragraph/list-item separator `\n` (or `\n\n` for a paragraph break) that lost its backslash the same way `u003c`/`u003e` did, sitting directly between two still-uncorrected `u003e`/`u003c` tokens. Confirmed via a regex scan (`/\\u003e(n+)\\u003c/g` against already-token-fixed content) to affect exactly 4 pages, 48 occurrences total: CropX Australia, New Zealand, Europe, and North America — all regional "Product Offering" pages, all inside the `cropx/two-column-alternating` block's `rows[].body` HTML string attribute.

**Fixed live first, via direct REST API repair** (same emergency-recovery pattern established in the Sep 10 incident — `window.wp.apiFetch` from a wp-admin browser tab, no new PHP tool needed to unblock Lauren immediately): for each of the 4 pages, replaced every `>(n+)<` run with `>` + one escaped `\n` per "n" found + `<`, validated the result with `JSON.parse()`, and saved via `POST /wp/v2/pages/<id>`. Verified live afterward — all 4 pages render cleanly with no stray "n"/"nn" text.

**Permanent fix added to `inc/unicode-escape-repair.php`.** Unlike the punctuation-adjacent-capital "nn" pattern (which has to guard against ordinary words like "canning" and is therefore held for manual review), a run of "n" characters sandwiched directly between an uncorrected `u003e` and `u003c` token is unambiguous — those tokens never occur in real prose — so it's now fixed automatically alongside the safe tokens, no review needed:

- `cropx_uer_escaped_newline_run_pattern()` — regex `/u003e(n+)u003c/`, matched against content *before* the general token fix runs (so it can still see the uncorrected `u003e`/`u003c` tokens as anchors).
- `cropx_uer_fix_escaped_newline_runs_string()` — comment-aware like every other fixer in this file: converts each "n" in the run to one escaped `\n`, leaves the surrounding `u003e`/`u003c` tokens untouched (the very next step converts those).
- `cropx_uer_apply_token_fixes()` now runs the new newline-run fix first, then the existing general token fix — order matters, since the newline-run fix depends on finding the tokens in their still-uncorrected form.
- The scan report and admin UI ("Safe tokens" section) now also surface newline-run hits with count + context, folded into the same auto-apply bucket as the other safe tokens.

**Shipped in `unicode-escape-repair-COMMENT-AWARE-FIX-v2.zip`** (replaces `inc/unicode-escape-repair.php` only — same no-revert-backup-needed situation as the v1 patch, since this only changes logic inside an already-guarded file). No further scan needed beyond the 4 pages already fixed live — this file-level fix is here so any *future* instance of this exact corruption pattern gets caught and fixed automatically the next time the tool's "Apply safe-token fixes" is run.

## September 10, 2026 — Unicode Escape Repair tool: INCIDENT — first fix run deleted content on 40+ posts, fully recovered, tool corrected

**What happened.** Earlier the same day, a sitewide unicode-escape corruption was diagnosed (see the two entries below this one) and a Tools → Unicode Escape Repair admin page was built and shipped to fix it. Lauren ran "Apply safe-token fixes." Shortly after, she reported: *"Oh no... this seems to have caused some elements to disappear completely."*

**Root cause.** The tool's first version restored every corrupted token (`u003c`, `u0022`, etc.) to its RAW character everywhere in `post_content`, via a blind `strtr()`. That's correct for a token sitting in ordinary text. But several of the corrupted strings lived *inside* a block's JSON attributes blob — e.g. a photo caption stored as `"caption":"...u003ca href=u0022...u0022...u003e..."` inside `<!-- wp:cropx/two-column-animated {...} -->`. Restoring a raw `"` there prematurely closes the JSON string; a raw `<`/`>` can corrupt the block comment's own `-->` boundary. WordPress's block parser silently drops attributes it can't parse as valid JSON — no error, no warning, anywhere. The very next time an affected post was touched (e.g. simply re-opening it in the block editor triggered an autosave), the broken attributes were discarded outright, and the block re-rendered on its `block.json` defaults — which is why the homepage's "See it in action" section showed generic placeholder copy and an empty photo area instead of its real content.

**Scope, found via a sitewide REST API scan** (every `post`/`page`/`cropx_publication`/`cropx_team_member`/`cropx_resource`/`cropx_dealer`/`cropx_testimonial`/`wp_block`, `context=edit` to read raw `post_content`, checking every `<!-- wp:cropx/... -->` block comment for a missing or JSON-invalid attributes blob): **42 posts/pages/patterns flagged**, including the homepage, several acquisition/product pages, and — most seriously — the **"General Contact Form" Synced Pattern**, used across 4 pages, which had lost its custom intro text, background color, and (most importantly) its Zoho `formActionUrl` entirely.

**Recovery — no data was permanently lost.** Every write this tool makes goes through `wp_update_post()`, which WordPress uses to auto-create a normal post revision before saving — so the pre-damage content was sitting in each post's Revisions history the whole time. Recovery process, run directly via the WP REST API (authenticated as Lauren's already-logged-in wp-admin session in the browser, so no new PHP tool was needed for the emergency recovery itself):
1. For each of the 42 flagged posts, walk its revision history (newest → oldest) to find the newest revision where **every** `cropx/*` block's JSON attributes are present and parse cleanly (this is the state from right before the bad fix ran — it still shows the *original* "u003c"-as-literal-text corruption, which is cosmetic and safe, just wrong-looking).
2. Re-apply a **corrected** version of the token fix to that revision's content (see below), producing content that's both fully restored *and* correctly rendered — not just rolled back to the ugly-but-intact pre-fix state.
3. Validate every block's JSON with `JSON.parse()` before saving; only save if 100% clean.
4. Save via `POST /wp/v2/<type>/<id>` (creates its own new revision automatically — nothing here bypasses or breaks the Revisions history).

39 of 42 were fixed by this automated pass. One — the "General Contact Form" pattern — needed a hand repair: its damage (a raw unescaped newline and raw quotes around a `mailto:` link inside the JSON, both invalid JSON syntax) predated the repair tool entirely and wasn't reversible by the token map, so it was manually re-escaped and validated the same way. The remaining 2 flagged items (the "Footer" pattern's `footer-brand` block, and one field on an internal "Utility Page: Background Color Testing" scratch page) turned out to be false positives — those blocks have *never* had custom attributes; they've always rendered on `block.json` defaults by design, which is a legitimate empty-attrs state, not damage.

**Final verification:** a full re-scan across all 8 post types after recovery came back with zero real damage. Spot-checked the homepage live — hero `<em>` renders correctly, the "Advisory & Dealer Platform" card's `&` renders correctly, and the "See it in action" → "Powerful Insights. Simple Decisions." section's 4 rotating photo+caption pairs are all back, with working links and images loading (200 OK) from the correct current domain.

**Permanent fix, shipped in `unicode-escape-repair-COMMENT-AWARE-FIX.zip`** (replaces `inc/unicode-escape-repair.php` only — `functions.php`'s `require` line from the original delivery is unchanged, no revert-backup needed since this only replaces logic inside an already-guarded, already-shipped file): both `cropx_uer_apply_token_fixes()` and `cropx_uer_apply_nn_fixes()` are now **comment-aware** via a new `cropx_uer_json_attr_spans()` helper that locates every block's JSON attributes span in the content. A match found *inside* one of those spans is restored to its **backslash-escaped** form (`u003c` → `<`, a literal double-newline → `\n\n`) instead of a raw character — always valid JSON, and WordPress decodes it to the real character normally when it reads the attribute, so the front end renders identically either way. A match found *outside* any block's JSON still gets the raw-character treatment as before, since that's just plain text. Full technical write-up lives in the file's own doc comments for the next person (or session) who touches it.

**Lesson:** on a "dynamic blocks only" theme (see CLAUDE.md gotcha #1), virtually all visible content lives inside JSON-in-HTML-comment attribute blobs, not plain post body text — any sitewide find-and-replace tool touching `post_content` on this site has to be block-comment-aware from the start, not just "does this string look unlikely to occur in real prose."

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho form: SHIPPED — removed County field (Zoho's own rule can never fire; my earlier addition was a mistake)

Follow-up to, and partial correction of, the entry directly below (FIX #2, which added County for California based on Zoho's Field Rules admin panel). Lauren tested the real live form (Country=US, State=California, City=Rancho Cordova) and sent a screenshot: no County field appears there at all. Our site's version of the same form, though, showed County with a Sacramento value selected — a real discrepancy between our replica and Zoho's actual behavior.

**Root cause, found by reading Zoho's own rule engine directly:** loaded the live form and read `window.zf_rule.ruleObjs` out of the page's own JS (the same object Zoho's client-side code uses to decide which rules fire). The "Show California Counties" rule (id `99543000000057271`) does exist, with condition `Dropdown8 EQUALS California`. But the real State field's live option value for California is `"California (CA)"`, not `"California"` — an exact-match rule against a value that never occurs. The rule is permanently dead on Zoho's own form, for every visitor, California residents included. This is a misconfiguration in Zoho's rule setup, not a platform limitation — and it's more authoritative than the Field Rules panel screenshots alone, since a rule can be defined there and still never fire.

The other three rules that shaped FIX #2 (State per-country, City US-only, the "Show States" OR-gate) were re-checked the same way and all have condition values that exactly match real field values — they fire correctly and are unaffected by this correction.

**Shipped:** removed the County field/row from both `cropx/zoho-form` and `cropx/zoho-contact-form` — `render.php` (County markup replaced with an explanatory comment), `view.js` (`syncCountyField()` and all county wiring removed), and doc comments in `inc/helpers.php` updated to explain why `cropx_zoho_california_county_list()` is kept but unused (in case Zoho ever fixes their rule's condition value). State/City logic (the confirmed-correct part of FIX #2) is untouched. Bumped `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.53`.

Delivered `zoho-remove-county-patch.zip` (targeted patch: `build/blocks/zoho-form/`, `build/blocks/zoho-contact-form/`, `functions.php`, `inc/helpers.php`) and `zoho-remove-county-REVERT-BACKUP.zip` (the pre-this-fix v0.4.52 state, County included — i.e. what FIX #2 shipped).

**Lesson:** the Field Rules admin panel lists rules as configured, not as they actually behave — `zf_rule.ruleObjs`, read straight from the live form's own JS, is the ground truth for whether a rule can ever fire. Worth checking there first for any future Zoho field-rule work rather than trusting the admin panel screenshot alone.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho form: SHIPPED — corrected field-rule scope (State required for 4 countries, not just US) + open CDN-delay question

Follow-up to the entry directly below (the cache-busting fix that got State/City to actually reach real visitors for the first time). Lauren reported two things once that fix went live: **(1)** a 1-2 second delay between selecting a country and the fields appearing, and **(2)** the fields were still US-only — Canada (and possibly others) needed them too but weren't getting them, meaning those countries' submissions were silently missing required data on Zoho's side.

**Delay (still open, needs a decision):** the delay is almost certainly the same ShortPixel CDN latency measured in the previous entry (1.4–4.5s CDN vs. 73–98ms origin for the same file) — `view.js` now actually reaches visitors (the point of the last fix), but it's arriving late enough that the native country `<select>` responds instantly while the JS-driven field reveal visibly lags behind it. Two options: turn off Settings → ShortPixel → WebP/AVIF & CDN → "Minify and serve the JavaScript files from the CDN" sitewide, or find a way to exclude just this file if ShortPixel supports per-file exclusions. Neither has been done yet — this needs Lauren's go-ahead since it's a sitewide account setting change.

**Field-rule scope — corrected:** my first pass (previous entries) concluded via an automated sweep of the live form that State/Province/Territory was US-only, and that County's trigger condition couldn't be determined. Both were wrong. The sweep checked the native `.required` DOM attribute, but Zoho doesn't use that to express requiredness — it's baked into static "* Required" label text and enforced by Zoho's own JS at submit time. The sweep also looped through many countries rapidly in one script execution, which produced stale/leaked visibility state rather than clean per-country reads. Lauren caught this by pulling up Zoho's own Field Rules admin panel directly (Fields → [Field] → Rules) and sending screenshots of every rule — the authoritative source, not a reverse-engineered guess. The real rules, each re-verified individually against the live form afterward:

- **"Show States"**: Country ∈ {United States, Canada, Australia, Mexico} → show + require State/Province/Territory (Dropdown8), with Zoho swapping in a different option list every time (50 US states, 13 Canadian provinces/territories, 6 Australian states, 32 Mexican states — all captured verbatim from the live form's own `<select>` options).
- **"Show City in USA"**: Country = United States → show + require City (SingleLine2). Narrower than State — US-only.
- **"Show California Counties"**: State/Province/Territory = "California (CA)" → show + require County (Dropdown7). Its 58-county option list was read directly out of the live DOM's `<option>` elements (present regardless of the field's visibility state, so this didn't need the rule to actually fire to capture it).

**Shipped:** `inc/helpers.php` gained four new list helpers (`cropx_zoho_canada_province_list()`, `cropx_zoho_australia_state_list()`, `cropx_zoho_mexico_state_list()`, `cropx_zoho_california_county_list()`) plus `cropx_zoho_state_options_by_country()`, a map consumed by both blocks. Both `cropx/zoho-form` and `cropx/zoho-contact-form` render.php files now emit a `data-state-options` JSON blob on the State field and a new County field/row (hidden by default). `view.js` in both blocks was rewritten: `syncGeoFields()` now shows/requires State for any of the four countries and *rebuilds* its option list from that JSON (not just toggles visibility — the options themselves change per country), shows/requires City only for US, and a separate `syncCountyField()` shows/requires County only when State's value is exactly "California (CA)" — a second-level dependency on State's value, not Country directly, so it's also re-checked on State's own `change` event. CSS in both blocks' style.css handles the case where only State is visible (non-US of the four) so it doesn't sit in a 2-column grid with an empty second cell.

Bumped `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.52`. Built and verified in a sandboxed copy (the connected repo folder's `node_modules` had read errors under `npm run build` this session — worked around by installing + building in `/tmp` and copying the block output back; unrelated to these code changes). Delivered `zoho-multicountry-fields-patch.zip` (targeted patch: `build/blocks/zoho-form/`, `build/blocks/zoho-contact-form/`, `functions.php`, `inc/helpers.php` — upload via WP File Manager per CLAUDE.md's targeted-patch convention) and `zoho-multicountry-fields-REVERT-BACKUP.zip` (pre-patch versions of the same files, rebuilt from the reverted source so the backup's build output is real and verified, not just copied-and-hoped).

**Not yet done:** no confirmed field rule found (or looked for) beyond these four countries + California — if Zoho adds more geography-gated fields later, the Field Rules panel is the reliable way to check, not another automated sweep.

<!-- last updated: September 10, 2026 (Zoho: SHIPPED FIX — script_loader_src filter ties viewScript cache-busting to CROPX_THEME_VERSION; real root cause was WP core preferring the build's own, non-changing asset hash over our version) -->

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho form: SHIPPED — real root cause found and fixed. Not ShortPixel alone; WordPress core's own script versioning was the deeper bug

Follow-up to the entry directly below (which correctly ruled out "County field" and identified ShortPixel's CDN as serving a stale `view.js`). Tried the obvious first fix — toggling Settings → ShortPixel → WebP/AVIF & CDN → "Minify and serve the JavaScript files from the CDN" off and back on, to force a re-cache. **That did not work**: re-fetched the CDN URL afterward and it was still the stale 2,075-byte file, same URL, same `?ver=` value as before. That ruled out "just a stuck CDN cache" and pointed at something keeping the URL itself from ever changing.

**Found it:** WordPress core's `register_block_script_handle()` (in `wp-includes/blocks.php`) reads a script's cache-busting version straight from `build/blocks/<block>/view.asset.php`'s own `'version'` key — a hash `@wordpress/scripts` generates at build time — and uses that *instead of* `$metadata['version']` whenever the key is present, which it always is for our blocks. So the `block_type_metadata` filter in `inc/enqueue.php` (the one from the Aug 2026 "curved hero" stale-CSS fix, see gotcha #10 in CLAUDE.md) only ever fixed style/editorStyle cache-busting — it has zero effect on scripts.

That alone would be harmless if the `.asset.php` hash reliably changed whenever a script's content changed. It didn't, here: confirmed the hash in `zoho-contact-form/view.js`'s `view.asset.php` was **identical** before and after the Sept 2026 Country=US patch (both builds have an empty `dependencies` array, and the hash appears to be derived from that dependency list rather than the bundled file's actual bytes). So the `<script>` URL WordPress put on the page literally never changed across the patch — meaning both real visitors' ordinary browser HTTP caches *and* the ShortPixel CDN (which keys its own cache off that exact URL) kept serving the pre-patch script indefinitely, with no way for either layer to know anything had changed. Toggling the ShortPixel setting couldn't have helped, because the URL it was asked to re-fetch was exactly the URL it already had cached.

**Shipped:** added a `script_loader_src` filter to `inc/enqueue.php` that rewrites the `ver` query arg to `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` for any script loaded from `wp-content/themes/cropx/build/blocks/` — overriding whatever WordPress core (or ShortPixel) would otherwise use, regardless of whether `@wordpress/scripts` happened to generate a new build hash. Bumped `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` to `0.4.51` in the same patch, which immediately forces a brand-new URL for every block's viewScript sitewide — a URL neither browsers nor ShortPixel's CDN have ever cached — resolving this specific bug and closing the gap for every future script patch, not just this one. From now on, bumping `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` (already standard practice on every deploy) is enough to bust script caches too, matching how it already works for CSS.

**Why this matters beyond Zoho:** this was a real, previously-unknown, sitewide gap — any past patch that touched a block's `view.js`/`edit.js` without also changing that block's declared script dependencies could have shipped correctly to the server while still being invisible to real visitors (and doubly so behind ShortPixel's CDN). Worth keeping an eye out for any other "I don't see my JS fix live" reports on older patches; this fix only prevents the problem going forward; it doesn't retroactively bust caches for anything patched before today.

**Delivered:** `zoho-cdn-cachebust-patch.zip` (`functions.php` + `inc/enqueue.php`) and `zoho-cdn-cachebust-REVERT-BACKUP.zip` (pre-edit versions, `CROPX_THEME_VERSION` back to `0.4.50`, filter removed). Deploy via WP File Manager as usual — upload into `wp-content/themes/cropx/`, extract in place. No `npm run build` needed; both changed files are plain PHP, not part of the webpack pipeline. After deploying, hard-refresh (or wait for a cache-buster to kick in) and re-check the Country=United States → State/City toggle on `/contact-us/europe/` or any other Zoho form page to confirm it now shows and requires those fields.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho form: CORRECTION — the "missing County field" diagnosis was wrong. Real bug: ShortPixel's JS-CDN is serving a stale, pre-patch view.js to real visitors

**This entry corrects the "County field" entry below it — that theory was wrong.** Lauren checked the real live Zoho form at `https://forms.zoho.eu/cropxx/form/CropXWebForm` directly and confirmed: selecting Country = United States reveals only **State/Province/Territory** and **City** — there is no visible County field at all. Live DOM inspection confirmed why: a `Dropdown7` select does exist in the form's HTML with California-county options, but its ancestor `.fieldWrapper` is permanently `display: none` regardless of Country/State — it's an inert, hidden legacy field on Zoho's own form, not something any visitor sees or needs to fill in, and not the cause of anything.

**What's actually happening, confirmed step by step:**
1. The Sept 2026 patch that added the Country=US → show/require State + City toggle (`syncUsOnlyFields()` in both `zoho-form/view.js` and `zoho-contact-form/view.js`) is real, correct, and genuinely deployed — confirmed by fetching `https://new.cropx.com/wp-content/themes/cropx/build/blocks/zoho-contact-form/view.js` directly from the origin: 7,791 bytes, contains `syncUsOnlyFields`.
2. But that's not the script real visitors' browsers actually load. The live page's `<script>` tag points at `https://spcdn.shortpixel.ai/spio/.../new.cropx.com/wp-content/themes/cropx/build/blocks/zoho-contact-form/view.js` — the site runs the **ShortPixel** plugin's "Minify and serve the JavaScript files from the CDN" feature (Settings → ShortPixel → WebP/AVIF & CDN), which proxies and caches JS (and CSS) through `spcdn.shortpixel.ai`.
3. Fetched that CDN URL directly: **2,075 bytes, no `syncUsOnlyFields` at all** — a stale copy from before this patch shipped. ShortPixel's CDN cache didn't pick up the origin change.
4. Net effect for a real US visitor: the browser loads the old script, so Country=United States never reveals or requires State/City. Their submission goes to Zoho with those fields blank, and Zoho's own endpoint silently drops/rejects it (or the record comes through fatally incomplete) — exactly reproducing every "United States fails, everything else works" result seen today, with no code bug involved at all.
5. This is a distinct caching layer from the one already ruled out earlier today (WordPress's own page-level `Cache-Control` headers, checked via `fetch('/', {cache:'no-store'})`) — that check was accurate but didn't cover ShortPixel's separate JS/CSS CDN, which is what was actually stale.

**Not yet fixed — needs a decision, not a code patch:** the fix here is almost certainly purging ShortPixel's CDN cache for the affected JS (and possibly CSS) files, not editing any block files — the code has been correct since the Sept 2026 UX patch shipped. Options to raise with Lauren: (a) find/trigger a cache-purge action in ShortPixel's settings, (b) temporarily toggle "Minify and serve the JavaScript files from the CDN" off then back on, or (c) contact ShortPixel support if no self-serve purge exists. Given this CDN sits in front of every patched JS/CSS file on the site, not just this form, it's worth sanity-checking whether other recent front-end patches have also been silently stuck on stale cached versions.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho URL-fix tool: found and fixed a real blind spot (Synced Patterns were never scanned) + auto-fixed a second, unrelated dead URL on the 404 page

Follow-up to the entry directly below. After the ZLbM0 URL-revert patch shipped, Lauren flagged that the scanning tool couldn't see "the page templates we CAN'T see here" — the 404 page, all archive pages (blog, results & research, news, ag insights), the Results CPT template, the standard post template, and all category/tag templates. Worth checking because most of the site's contact-form instances on those templates aren't hardcoded in PHP — they're inserted via WordPress Synced Patterns (`cropx_get_synced_block_ref()`), referenced from 12+ PHP templates (`404.php`, `page-results.php`, `page-insights.php`, `page-news.php`, `home.php`, `single-cropx_publication.php`, both taxonomy templates, `archive-cropx_resource.php`, `archive-cropx_team_member.php`, `archive-cropx_dealer.php`, `single-cropx_dealer.php`, `single-cropx_team_member.php`, `single-cropx_resource.php`, `footer.php`, `page-thank-you.php`).

**Real bug found:** `cropx_zoho_fix_scan()`'s `WP_Query` used `'post_type' => 'any'`. In WordPress, `'any'` does **not** mean "every post type" — it silently excludes any post type registered with `exclude_from_search => true`, and WordPress core's own `wp_block` post type (which is what Synced Patterns actually are under the hood) is registered exactly that way. So the tool had never once been capable of scanning or fixing a formperma URL living inside a Synced Pattern — a real, previously-unknown gap, not a caching or deploy issue.

**Fix:** swapped the query to `get_post_types( array(), 'names' )`, which returns every registered post type regardless of `exclude_from_search`. The tool now finds and fixes matches inside Synced Patterns (`wp_block` posts) exactly the same as it does regular posts and pages.

**Live spot-check across all 12 pattern-driven templates** (reading the rendered `.zcf-form`/`.zform-form` DOM `action` attribute directly on each live page) found every one correct **except the 404 page**, which was on a completely different, unrelated dead URL — the block's own raw factory-default "TestForm" ID (`RtEXjBYn9Ze3_OId13J2g9L2CoXcDpCSZ76-LATKQWs`), never pointed at CropXWebForm at all. This had been flagged in the original version of the tool as "confirmed dead but not auto-fixed, for manual review" — now confirmed exactly where it lives (404 page only) and the tool auto-fixes it too.

**Shipped:** `inc/fix-zoho-formperma.php` now scans posts, pages, *and* Synced Patterns, and replaces **two** outdated URLs — the `ce5fr...` URL (this morning's wrong fix) and the "TestForm" default — both with the confirmed-working `ZLbM0...` URL, in one Preview/Apply pass. Delivered as `zoho-wpblock-scan-fix-patch.zip` (forward) and `zoho-wpblock-scan-fix-REVERT-BACKUP.zip` (rollback to the ZLbM-revert-only version from the entry below). Same deploy path: WP File Manager → upload into `wp-content/themes/cropx/` → extract in place → Tools → Zoho Form URL Fix → Preview, then Apply. This run will additionally catch and fix the 404 page, which the previous run could not touch.

**Not yet done:** County (`Dropdown7`) field for US submissions — see below, still open.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho contact form: formperma URL mystery solved — reverted back to the ORIGINAL ID; real remaining bug is a missing County field for US submissions only

Follow-up to the "Access Denied" entry directly below. Short version: this morning's "fix" (swapping the dead-looking `ZLbM0...` ID for `ce5fr...`) was wrong. The original `ZLbM0...` ID is the correct one after all — it was never actually dead, and this whole detour cost real time because of a bad test methodology.

**What actually happened, reconstructed:**
1. `ZLbM0EIkXT0X5LHuq81beYTS__PnpLfoHsZgHIRVvmU` was assumed dead because directly navigating to `https://forms.zohopublic.eu/.../formperma/ZLbM0.../` (no `/htmlRecords/submit`) returns "Sorry! Page not found." Tested repeatedly, including from a completely fresh browser session with no prior cookies/history, and it was consistent every time.
2. **This was the wrong test.** Zoho apparently validates a formperma link differently depending on whether it's being GET-loaded as a standalone page versus POSTed to as a form's submit target — a link can be disabled/invalid for direct viewing while still working correctly as a `htmlRecords/submit` target. Nothing in this investigation had reason to know that going in; it only became clear once directly contradicted by real evidence.
3. Lauren's coworker (Larissa) independently used the original `ZLbM0...` ID and got a real Zoho record — verified directly in Zoho's admin (screenshot: Referrer Name `https://new.cropx.com/`, Added Time `10-Sep-2026 01:07:43`, CRM Status "New Record"). Lauren reproduced this herself afterward with Country = "Guam."
4. Lauren *also* tested with `ZLbM0...` and it failed for her — but with Country = **"United States"** specifically. That's the tell: switching only the country from "United States" to "Guam," same page, same URL, same everything else, flipped the result from fail to success.

**Real conclusion:** the formperma URL was never the bug. `ZLbM0EIkXT0X5LHuq81beYTS__PnpLfoHsZgHIRVvmU` is correct and has apparently been correct the whole time. The actual, still-open bug is exactly what was flagged and deliberately deferred in this morning's field-schema fix: **County (`Dropdown7`) is required by Zoho's live form whenever State is set (i.e., whenever Country = "United States"), and our blocks still don't send it.** Every failure anyone has hit today with a "United States" test is consistent with this one gap. Every non-US test (Netherlands, Guam, Lauren's original Australia-page tests, etc.) sails through fine because it never touches the State/County/City field rule at all.

**Shipped:** reverted `inc/fix-zoho-formperma.php` to swap `ce5frCZ7R0LQudIddSDJCibZ2vvWyfJX9WJ6UqR_ApU` → back to → `ZLbM0EIkXT0X5LHuq81beYTS__PnpLfoHsZgHIRVvmU` (the tool's swap direction is now the reverse of what it did this morning; same admin page, same usage — Tools → Zoho Form URL Fix → Preview, then Apply). Doc comments in the file rewritten to explain the GET-vs-POST validation distinction and the County gap, so a future reader doesn't repeat this detour. No other file hardcodes either URL — confirmed by grep across `render.php`/`block.json`/`edit.js` for both blocks; the only other formperma reference is the two blocks' own unrelated `block.json` default ("TestForm," a different, unrelated form, already flagged separately and untouched by this tool).

**Delivered:** `zoho-url-revert-to-zlbm-patch.zip` (just `inc/fix-zoho-formperma.php`) and `zoho-url-revert-to-zlbm-REVERT-BACKUP.zip` (the pre-edit version — i.e., this morning's ce5fr-swapping version — for exact rollback if needed). Deploy the same way as prior patches: upload into `wp-content/themes/cropx/` via WP File Manager, extract in place, then run the tool again from Tools → Zoho Form URL Fix (Preview first, then Apply) to swap every page's content back to the correct URL.

**Not yet done — the actual next fix:** implement County (`Dropdown7`) for the US-only case. Last attempt to trigger Zoho's State→County cascade via simulated DOM `change` events on the live form didn't work; next approach is to watch actual network traffic while manually selecting a State on the live Zoho-hosted form, in case County's option list is fetched via an API call rather than being pre-rendered — that would explain why a synthetic event never populated it. Until this ships, US-country submissions through our form will continue to silently fail even with the correct URL in place.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho contact form: field-schema fix confirmed deployed and correct, but a NEW, deeper blocker found — Zoho's own submit endpoint returns "Access Denied"

Lauren reported "still nothing coming through" after the field-schema patch (below) was deployed. Re-investigated from scratch rather than assuming the patch didn't ship.

**Step 1 — confirmed the patch genuinely is live.** Inspected the actual homepage's embedded Zoho form on `new.cropx.com` directly via JS (`document.querySelectorAll('.zcf-form, .zform-form')`) and read every field's `name` attribute off the live DOM. All the corrected names are present and correct: `Dropdown` (Country, was the dead `Dropdown1`), `Dropdown8` + `SingleLine2` (State/City, new), `Dropdown3` (Source, was colliding with Country's real key). The Form Action URL is also confirmed correct (Lauren separately screenshotted the block editor's Zoho Connection panel and asked to confirm — yes, matches). **This rules out "the patch never deployed" as the explanation for continued failures.**

**Step 2 — ran a real, permitted test submission and, critically, this time read Zoho's actual response instead of just the HTTP status.** Every prior test in this investigation only ever confirmed a `200` status code from the browser's Network panel — but that was never proof of acceptance, since a hidden-iframe cross-origin POST can't be read by our JS either way, and Zoho is known to return `200` even for a rejected submission. This time: filled the live homepage form with dummy data via JS, then temporarily removed the form's `target` attribute so the tab would navigate top-level straight to Zoho's real response page instead of into the invisible hidden iframe. The result:

> **Zoho's actual response body: "Sorry! Access Denied."** (with a "Click here to go back" link) — served with an HTTP 200 status, which is exactly why every previous "200 = should be fine" check missed this.

This means Zoho itself is rejecting the submission at the door, before any field-level validation even runs — a completely different (and more fundamental) failure than the field-schema mismatch fixed earlier today. The field-schema fix was real and necessary, but it was fixing a second bug, not *the* bug that's been preventing entries the whole time.

**What "Access Denied" most likely means:** Zoho Forms has a form-level setting (Share → Embed → Embed Settings → Embed Location) that can restrict which domains are allowed to submit to a form: "Anywhere," "Deny Embedding," or an "Allowed Domains" list (max 5 domains) — [Zoho's own docs](https://help.zoho.com/portal/en/kb/forms/embedding-forms/embed-settings/articles/embed-settings). A prior pass in this same investigation (see the entry below) checked this exact setting in Zoho's admin UI and found it set to **"Anywhere"** — which should mean no domain is blocked. That's now in direct tension with the "Access Denied" response just observed, so one of these is true:

1. There's a **separate** access-control layer specific to the raw `htmlRecords/submit` POST endpoint (the "Export as HTML/CSS" integration method) that isn't the same thing as "Embed Location" — e.g. some Zoho accounts have an additional CSRF/referrer check tied to the exported HTML's hidden fields (`zf_referrer_name` is currently blank in our markup — worth checking whether it's supposed to carry a specific token value from Zoho's own export, not just an analytics label).
2. **This is a false alarm caused by the test methodology, not a real block on real visitors** — this session ran several rapid automated test submissions back-to-back from a cloud sandbox's IP address in a short window (this test plus the two from earlier today), and Zoho (or a WAF in front of it) may be temporarily flagging that specific IP/pattern as automated/bot traffic rather than blocking the domain itself. A real visitor's browser, from a normal residential/office IP, hitting the form once, may not trigger this at all.

**This has not been resolved — next step is on Lauren's side, not something to keep guessing at from here:** the fastest way to tell these two apart is for Lauren to submit one real test entry herself, from her own normal browser, and check whether it also lands in Zoho's "All Entries" or not.
- If Lauren's own manual submission **works**: this was rate-limiting/bot-detection triggered by this session's repeated automated tests, real visitors are unaffected, and the field-schema fix shipped today is the whole fix.
- If Lauren's own manual submission **also fails / also shows "Access Denied" or a similar rejection**: this is a real, ongoing Zoho-side access-control setting (likely tied to the `htmlRecords/submit` / HTML-export method specifically, separate from "Embed Location") that needs to be found and fixed in Zoho's admin, most likely by Naomi (who owns/shares `CropXWebForm`) — worth opening a Zoho support ticket citing the exact "Sorry! Access Denied" response text if the setting can't be found in the UI.

**Diagnostic test entries left in place from today's testing** (none of which created Zoho records, consistent with everything above): two dummy submissions via the homepage form (First: "ClaudeTest" / "ClaudeTest2", Last: "Diagnostic" / "Diagnostic2", emails `lauren.hostetter+claudetest@cropx.com` / `+claudetest2@cropx.com`), plus the one from the earlier field-schema investigation (First: "Claude", Last: "DiagnosticTest").

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho contact form: REAL root cause found — live form schema has drifted from what our blocks send (formperma fix alone was not enough)

After the formperma URL fix below, Lauren reported submissions were *still* not coming through. Since the hidden-iframe submit pattern always shows an optimistic success message regardless of what Zoho actually does with the POST (see below), a real diagnosis required bypassing the UI entirely.

**Investigation, in order:**
1. Got explicit permission from Lauren and submitted one real dummy test entry through the live Australia contact-us page. The POST returned a genuine HTTP 200 from Zoho's own submit endpoint, and the front end showed the success message.
2. Checked Zoho's admin (`forms.zoho.eu`) for that entry. Initially concluded Lauren's Zoho account had no access to `CropXWebForm` at all — this was wrong, caused by a Zoho Forms SPA bug where navigating straight to the `#sharedforms` hash doesn't reliably trigger the app to re-render that view (it kept showing "My Forms," which only has a "Test Form"). Lauren corrected this — the form is listed under **Shared Forms** (owned by naomi.cerny@cropx.com, shared with Lauren) — confirmed once by actually clicking the sidebar link instead of navigating by URL.
3. With the real form's admin panel open, checked every entries bucket: All Entries (1,805 total, newest from Sep 9), Partially Saved (0), Trashed (1, unrelated), Integration – Failed (4, unrelated). The dummy test entry is in **none of them**. All 1,805 visible entries show `Referrer Name: https://cropx.com/` — i.e. **every single real entry came from the OLD site's integration**, not the new one.
4. Ruled out: Embed Location restriction (set to "Anywhere"), the "Allow submissions from HTML/CSS-embedded sources" toggle (confirmed ON), caching/CDN serving a stale URL (confirmed via an anonymous no-cookie fetch of the live page), and the formperma ID itself (confirmed to match Zoho's own official permalink for this exact form, character-for-character).
5. Zoho's own "Download HTML/CSS" export downloads a zip to the real filesystem, which isn't reachable from this environment — so instead, navigated directly to the form's own live public URL (`forms.zohopublic.eu/.../formperma/...`) and inspected the actual rendered DOM/field names with JS, which is authoritative and didn't require a download at all.

**Root cause, confirmed:** the live `CropXWebForm` form's field schema has changed since our blocks (`cropx/zoho-form`, `cropx/zoho-contact-form`) were built against it, and our `render.php` field set is now out of sync in two ways:

- **Two wrong field names** (fields exist on both sides, but the POST key is wrong so Zoho silently drops the value): our form posts Country as `Dropdown1` — the real field name is `Dropdown`. Our form posts the "how did you hear about us" source field as `Dropdown` — but that name is actually Country's real field name on Zoho's side, so our "source" answer is silently landing in Zoho's Country field instead.
- **Three fields our form never asks for or sends, and all three are marked required on the live Zoho form**: `Dropdown8` (State/Province/Territory), `Dropdown7` (County — populated with California county names specifically), and `SingleLine2` (City). A raw HTML/CSS POST that's missing a server-required field is exactly the kind of submission Zoho silently discards — consistent with every symptom observed: genuine 200 response (Zoho accepted the HTTP request), but no record anywhere (Zoho's server-side validation rejected the data), and total invisibility on our end (the hidden-iframe pattern can't read Zoho's cross-origin response to know validation failed).

Confirmed field names/requiredness directly from the live form's DOM (`em.important` marker + `mandatory` attribute), not guessed:

| Zoho field name | Label | Required | In our render.php? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Name_First` / `Name_Last` | First/Last Name | Yes | ✅ matches |
| `Email` | Email | Yes | ✅ matches |
| `Dropdown` | Country | Yes | ❌ we send `Dropdown1` (dead key) |
| `Dropdown2` | What is your role? | Yes | ✅ matches |
| `Dropdown8` | State/Province/Territory | **Yes** | ❌ missing entirely |
| `Dropdown7` | County | **Yes** | ❌ missing entirely |
| `SingleLine2` | City | **Yes** | ❌ missing entirely |
| `PhoneNumber` | Phone | No | ⚠️ we send `PhoneNumber_countrycode` — needs re-verification |
| `MultiLine` | How can we help? | No | ✅ matches |
| `Dropdown3` | How did you hear about us? | No | ❌ we send `Dropdown` (collides with Country's real key) |
| `SingleLine` | Other source | No | not sent (fine, optional) |
| `DecisionBox` | Marketing consent | No | ✅ matches |
| `SingleLine1` | Source Website | No | not sent (fine, optional) |

**This has not been fixed yet — flagged to Lauren for a decision before building anything**, because there are two genuinely different paths and they trade off differently:

1. **Patch render.php to match today's schema** — add the 3 missing fields (State/Province/Territory, County, City) to both Zoho blocks' UI and POST payload, fix the 2 wrong field names. Keeps our on-brand custom styling. But: it's fixing a symptom, not the disease — this is the second time the Zoho-side form definition has drifted out from under our hardcoded field list (first the formperma URL, now the whole field set), and Zoho's own "Export as HTML & CSS" panel explicitly warns "field rules... will not be supported" for this integration method, meaning if Country/State/County ever become conditionally required (the County field's California-only option list suggests it might only make sense for US submissions), our static form can't replicate that logic and will break again.
2. **Switch both blocks to Zoho's iframe "Embed in your page" method** — the same method the *old* cropx.com site uses, which is exactly why its 1,800 real entries all work reliably. Zoho always serves the current live field definition and its own field-rule logic inside the iframe, so it can never drift out of sync again. Trade-off: loses some of the custom CropX-styled form UI (Zoho's iframe can be lightly themed via their own Theme editor, but not fully matched to our design tokens), and re-introduces the exact bfcache/frozen-submit-button bug that the hidden-iframe pattern was originally built to avoid (documented in the block's own code comments) — though that bug applied to a *top-level-navigation* iframe pattern; worth re-checking whether it still applies to a passive always-visible iframe embed rather than a form that navigates on submit.

Also still unconfirmed either way: whether `County` being required is really universal or a Zoho-side field-rule mistake (US county names for a company with an EU/AU/global customer base looks like it may itself be a misconfiguration worth flagging back to Naomi/whoever owns the Zoho form, independent of what we do on the WordPress side).

**Diagnostic test entry left in place:** one dummy submission (First: "Claude", Last: "DiagnosticTest", Email: `diagnostic-test-delete-me@cropx.com`) was submitted through the live Australia contact form during this investigation. As expected given the root cause above, it did **not** create a Zoho record, so there's nothing to delete there — but worth noting in case it surfaces anywhere unexpected.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho contact form: field-schema fix shipped (Lauren chose "patch the fields")

Lauren reviewed the two options above and chose to patch the fields rather than switch to Zoho's iframe embed, keeping the current on-brand styling. Asked specifically whether this would change the form's appearance — answer: no, existing fields are untouched; the two new fields (US visitors only) use the exact same input/select styling already in place, so the form just gets a little taller for that one segment of visitors.

**What shipped:**
- **Fixed the two wrong field names** (universal — fixes every country, since Country wasn't being received by Zoho at all before this): Country now posts as `Dropdown` (was the dead key `Dropdown1`). The "How did you hear about us?" field now posts as `Dropdown3` (was `Dropdown` — which is actually Country's real key, so a visitor's source answer was silently overwriting Country before this fix).
- **Added State/Province/Territory (`Dropdown8`) and City (`SingleLine2`)** to both `cropx/zoho-form` and `cropx/zoho-contact-form`. Confirmed directly on the live form (selecting Country = "United States" and watching the DOM) that Zoho only shows and requires these two fields for that one country — a field rule. `view.js` in both blocks replicates the same behavior: a new `.zcf-row--us-only` / `.zform-row--us-only` row, rendered `hidden` by default, becomes visible and `required` only when the visitor picks "United States" as Country, and is cleared + hidden again if they change their mind. A new `cropx_zoho_us_state_list()` helper in `inc/helpers.php` (same pattern as the existing `cropx_zoho_country_list()`) supplies the 50-state option list, copied verbatim from Zoho's own live dropdown.
- **Deliberately left out: County (`Dropdown7`).** It's present and required on the live form once a State is selected, but its option list is itself state-dependent (cascading — e.g. selecting California populates a list of California counties), and repeated attempts to trigger that cascade directly on the live form (native `change` events, jQuery `.trigger('change')`) never made it appear, so its real trigger condition couldn't be confirmed. Rather than guess and ship a broken replica, it's omitted — flagged as a follow-up below.
- **CSS fix needed for the new hidden row to actually hide**: `.zcf-row`/`.zform-row` already sets `display: grid` at the same specificity as the browser's built-in `[hidden]` rule, and since ours loads after the UA stylesheet, source order would let it win and show an empty grid row. Added `.zcf-row[hidden] { display: none }` / `.zform-row[hidden] { ... }` (compound selector, higher specificity) to both blocks' `style.css` to fix this before it could ever ship broken.
- `getRequiredEls()` in both `view.js` files now re-queries `[required]` fresh every time (submit, and initial listener setup) instead of caching the list once at page load, since the State/City fields' `required` attribute now changes at runtime based on Country.

**Why the field mismatch existed in the first place:** never fully resolved — either the Zoho form was edited (fields renamed/added) after these blocks were originally built against it, or they were built against the wrong reference form entirely. Not pursued further since the live field set is now the source of truth either way.

**Build tooling note:** `npm run build` (wp-scripts) fails in this environment with a low-level "Resource deadlock avoided" error — a sandbox limitation, not a problem with the code (verified separately with `node --check` on both `view.js` files, which passed clean, and manual brace/`<?php`-tag balance checks on all edited PHP files, which all balanced). Since the build tool couldn't run, `build/blocks/{zoho-form,zoho-contact-form}/render.php` and `view.js` were updated by copying the edited `src/` versions directly (`render.php` is copied byte-for-byte by the real build too, so this is exactly what a real build would produce there; `view.js` ends up unminified instead of terser-minified — functionally identical, just a few KB larger over the wire, not worth blocking the fix on). The two `style-index.css`/`style-index-rtl.css` files got a single minified rule appended rather than a full rebuild, since the new CSS has no LTR/RTL-directional properties to need real RTLCSS processing.

**Files changed:** `inc/helpers.php` (new `cropx_zoho_us_state_list()`), `src/blocks/zoho-form/render.php`, `src/blocks/zoho-form/view.js`, `src/blocks/zoho-form/style.css`, `src/blocks/zoho-contact-form/render.php`, `src/blocks/zoho-contact-form/view.js`, `src/blocks/zoho-contact-form/style.css`, plus the matching `build/blocks/...` copies, and `functions.php` (`CROPX_THEME_VERSION` bumped 0.4.49 → 0.4.50 to bust the cache on the changed CSS/JS).

**Delivered:** `zoho-field-schema-fix-patch.zip` (all the files above, forward) and `zoho-field-schema-fix-revert-backup.zip` (the exact pre-patch versions of every one of those files — reconstructed precisely by reversing each edit, and spot-verified byte-for-byte identical in size to the pre-patch originals for every file where an original size was available to compare against). Deploy the same way as previous small patches: upload into `wp-content/themes/cropx/` via WP File Manager and extract, overwriting in place. If anything looks wrong afterward, upload the revert-backup zip the same way to go back to exactly how it was before this patch (including the formperma-fix version bump staying at 0.4.49 → back to what it was).

**Not yet done / flagged to Lauren:**
- **County (`Dropdown7`) is not implemented** — see above. If a US submission with a filled State is still silently rejected by Zoho (the same failure signature as before: front-end shows success, nothing lands in Zoho), County not being sent is the next thing to check. Worth also asking whoever owns the Zoho form (Naomi?) directly whether County is supposed to be required at all — a California-only county list for a company with international/multi-state customers looks like it could itself be a misconfiguration on the Zoho side, independent of anything on our end.
- **No end-to-end test submission run yet** against this specific fix (both a non-US and a US test, to confirm the Country/Source fix alone resolves the ~99% non-US case, and separately that a US submission with State + City filled in actually lands in Zoho's records). Strongly recommended before considering this closed — same diagnostic pattern as before (real dummy submission, then check Zoho's "All Entries" for it).
- Build tooling (`npm run build`) is non-functional in this sandbox — see note above. Worth a real `npm run build && rsync` from Lauren's own machine at some point to get proper minified `view.js`/CSS into `build/`, though the current unminified versions work correctly as-is.

## September 10, 2026 — Zoho contact form: fixed dead formperma URL (submissions were silently failing sitewide)

Lauren reported Zoho form submissions weren't coming through, starting from the Australia contact-us page. Investigation (direct browser navigation to the form's own formperma URL, cross-checked against both the `.eu` and `.com` Zoho data centers) confirmed the URL baked into the site's `cropx/zoho-contact-form` and `cropx/zoho-form` blocks returns Zoho's own "Sorry! Page not found." — a dead endpoint, not a WordPress or plugin issue. Because the form posts into a hidden iframe by design (see the doc comment at the top of `render.php` — this avoids a bfcache/frozen-button failure mode from an earlier version) and shows an optimistic success message as soon as client-side validation passes, this failure was completely invisible on the front end; nothing was ever actually reaching Zoho.

**Root cause:** the block's `formActionUrl` attribute (stored per-instance in each page's `post_content`, since this is a dynamic block — see CLAUDE.md convention #1) pointed at formperma ID `ZLbM0EIkXT0X5LHuq81beYTS__PnpLfoHsZgHIRVvmU`, which is dead. Lauren compared against the *old* cropx.com site's working Elementor embed for the same `CropXWebForm` / `cropxx` / `.eu` form and found it uses a different, confirmed-working ID: `ce5frCZ7R0LQudIddSDJCibZ2vvWyfJX9WJ6UqR_ApU` — verified by direct navigation (real Zoho form loads, fields match the block's field set exactly) and by Lauren's own confirmation that it works on the live site.

Initially scoped this fix to just the 6 contact-us pages, but Lauren flagged that the Zoho form block is used on nearly every page of the new site (posts, archive pages, etc.), not just contact-us — so a per-page editor fix doesn't scale. Built a sitewide fix instead.

**Fix:** `inc/fix-zoho-formperma.php` — a new Tools → "Zoho Form URL Fix" admin page (manage_options only). Scans every post/page of every post type and status for the exact dead formperma submit URL string anywhere in `post_content`, and replaces it with the corrected URL. This is a full-string exact match (not a bare ID swap), so it's scoped tightly to this one known-bad value and can't touch anything else in a page's content. "Preview changes" runs read-only with a table of affected posts; "Apply changes" saves. Safe to re-run.

It also separately **reports (read-only, does not touch)** any post still using the block's own default formperma URL (the "TestForm" ID, `RtEXjBYn9Ze3_OId13J2g9L2CoXcDpCSZ76-LATKQWs` — also confirmed dead, used e.g. on the 404 page's contact block) — its correct replacement hasn't been confirmed with Lauren yet, so this tool flags it for a decision rather than guessing.

Wired into `functions.php` behind the same `file_exists()` guard as the other small patches.

**Files changed:** `inc/fix-zoho-formperma.php` (new), `functions.php` (added guarded require).
**Delivered:** `zoho-formperma-fix-patch-final.zip` (new file + updated functions.php) and `zoho-formperma-fix-revert-backup-final.zip` (original functions.php only).

**Not yet done / flagged to Lauren:**
- The "TestForm" formperma ID (404 page and possibly elsewhere) is a separate, still-dead endpoint whose correct replacement isn't confirmed — the tool reports where it's used but doesn't touch it.
- The corrected URL's `/htmlRecords/submit` suffix follows Zoho's standard "Export as HTML & CSS" REST submit pattern (same pattern the dead URL used), which should carry over correctly to the new ID since it's the same underlying `CropXWebForm` — but this wasn't confirmed via the old site's own integration (which used Zoho's iframe "Embed in your page" method instead, a different URL). **Recommended next step: after applying, submit one real test through a fixed form and confirm the lead actually lands in Zoho**, to close the loop on both the fix and this assumption.
- `php -l` isn't available in this sandbox — reviewed manually for brace/syntax balance instead, same limitation as the Media URL Cleanup tool below.
